| Norm drift (L=64, m=0.3, 200 steps) | $5.68\times10^{-14}$ | $<10^{-12}$ |
| D1 zitterbewegung (L=256, n_steps=2000, m=0.5) | $\omega = 1.04877$ vs $\pi/3 = 1.04720$ (0.15%, within FFT bin) | within FFT bin |

## 2026-05-17 (new design note — emergent-time roadmap)

Added `ca-emergent-time-plan.md`. Proposes a reinterpretation of the v2 stack in which the global update index $n$ is bookkeeping and physical time is the per-cell tick counter $N(\mathbf{x})$ of nontrivial state transitions. No code changes; design-only entry.

Six phases T0–T5. T0 is a proposition file; T1 is a lazy-update wrapper that increments $N$ only on cells whose state changes above an FFT-floor threshold; T2 re-derives group velocity, Shapiro delay, and redshift in tick language and checks equality against the global-$n$ derivation; T3 is the large optional item — partitioned (Margolus-style) BCC update with unitarity proven along causal sequences; T4 reformulates energy conservation and the Paper 4 DSR boost on tick-foliated frames; T5 lists falsifiable predictions unique to the tick reading (vacuum cells experiencing zero proper time exactly, lazy-run scaling with packet volume, asymmetric tick clocks as a direct probe of $\phi$).

First decision point is **after T2.B (Shapiro tick-ratio)**: if $|\Delta\tau_{\text{global}} - \Delta\tau_{\text{tick}}|/\Delta\tau_{\text{global}} < 10^{-12}$, the emergent-time reading is consistent with v2 and the rest proceeds. Otherwise the discrepancy itself is the new finding.

Preservation contract: every F-phase gate and every dimensionless lattice test must still pass at its current residual floor — the roadmap is additive, layering a second reading on the same propagator rather than replacing it. T3 is the only item that would touch the propagator itself, and is explicitly gated on T1+T2 succeeding first.

Affected docs (this entry only): `ca-emergent-time-plan.md` added; `docs/status/project-status.md` Progress table extended.

## 2026-05-17 (decision point — SI identification of $(a,\tau)$ is currently undefined)

No code or test changes. Documents a decision the project has so far left implicit: the Weyl-QCA lattice light speed $c_\text{lat} = 1/\sqrt d$ is dimensionless (cells per tick), and converting any lattice quantity to SI requires committing to a specific lattice spacing $a$ in metres and tick duration $\tau$ in seconds. The naive choice $a = \ell_P$, $\tau = t_P$ predicts a measured speed of light $c_\text{physical} = c/\sqrt d$ — in 3D, that is $1.732 \times 10^{8}$ m/s, **0.5774× the observed $c$**, an exact $\sqrt 3$ shortfall.

Full analysis in `findings.md` Finding 10. The mismatch traces to a definitional consequence of Planck units ($\ell_P/t_P = c$ exactly), not to any numerical residual. Three internally consistent resolutions, mutually exclusive:

1. Keep $a = \ell_P$, set $\tau = t_P/\sqrt d$ (tick smaller than Planck time by $\sqrt d$).
2. Keep $\tau = t_P$, set $a = \ell_P\sqrt d$ (cell larger than Planck length by $\sqrt d$).
3. Reinterpret $a/\tau$ as the lattice lightcone (maximum signal speed = $c\sqrt d$), with physical $c \equiv (a/\tau)/\sqrt d$ being what particles propagate at. Retires the convention $\ell_P/t_P = c$.

Status of existing tests: **all 30/30 dimensionless lattice tests are unaffected** — they live in lattice units and never invoke $a$ or $\tau$. The $\sqrt d$ factor enters only at the SI conversion boundary. Specifically: L1–L4 unitarity, dispersion residuals, norm drift; F1–F4 unification gates; D1/E1/E2 phase tests; and the 8-row exact-algebraic inventory in `docs/theory/ca-reference.md` are all dimensionally pure.

What this rules in / out for future work:

- Any future lattice-to-SI absolute-magnitude calculation must declare which of the three resolutions is in force.
- Finding 8's L4 lensing absolute-coefficient extension (currently checking only the ratio $\Delta(2M)/\Delta(M) \to 2$ within $3.5\times 10^{-3}$) acquires an explicit $\sqrt d$ factor when implemented.
- F3b deflection magnitude in cells does not need an immediate decision; the $1/b$ scaling test (item 12) is dimensionally pure.
- No code change is required *now*; the decision is logged so the next person mapping a lattice number to a SI prediction does not silently pick a resolution.

Affected docs (this entry only): `findings.md` Finding 10 added; `docs/theory/ca-reference.md` exactness table updated with $c_\text{lat} = 1/\sqrt d$ and the SI-mapping $\sqrt d$ factor; `docs/theory/ca-reference.md` "Current limitations" extended with the SI-identification flag.

## 2026-05-17 (new design note — electroweak mass-generation paths)

Added `ca-electroweak-design.md`. Re-organizes content already in `docs/theory/ca-unified-v2.md` into three named mass-generation paths and compares each against the three-mass framework in `ostoma-trushyk-1999-summary.md` §7. No code or test changes.

Three v2 paths identified: **A** = Yukawa $m_{\text{eff}} = y\,\text{Re}(\Phi)$ (S4, F1/F2/F4 gates, zero new engineering); **B** = total stress-energy sourcing the EMQG potential $\phi$ via Paper 6 Eq. 19.7 (S1, V11/V12/F3b gates, ~1 week engineering on a time-dependent Poisson solver — flagged as v2's largest implementation risk); **C** = composite-photon $E/c^2$ effective mass via Paper 1 Eq. 35 (S3, V4/E1 gates, ~4–6 days for new `ca_maxwell.py`).

Mapping to OT: Path B ↔ OT gravitational mass $m_g$ (direct correspondence — same modified Poisson equation). Path C ↔ OT inertial mass $m_i$ (partial — same observables, but v2 has lattice photons without the masseon/charged-virtual-vacuum that QI requires). Path A ↔ no OT analog (OT has no Higgs scalar; rest mass in OT emerges from EM-vacuum coupling, not a fundamental scalar). OT's low-level "mass charge" has no v2 analog — v2 has no gravitons, so the subdominant gravity channel and the predicted $10^{-40}$ WEP violation are absent. v2 collapses OT's "$m_g$ = photon-vacuum part + graviton-mass-charge part" into a single stress-energy → $\phi$ source.

The legacy `test_L3` is kept as an alias to `test_L3a` so external callers don't break. The driver now prints `L3a STATUS: PASS (kinematic)` and `L3b STATUS: PARTIAL (Maxwell curl residual is O(k))`. This ends the misleading "3/3 PASS + 1 INFO" framing that hid the central Maxwell identity failure.

Richardson ratios 4.05 and 4.01 — residual is exactly $O(\Delta t^2)$, i.e. the Dirac CA satisfies the discrete continuity identity at the order of the integrator.

  (b) SU(2)-coupled stepper: the isospin rotation moves charge between ν and e components.  Total local $\rho = |\eta_\nu|^2 + |\eta_e|^2 + |\chi_e|^2$ should be invariant pointwise.  Measured drift 4.4e-16 — exact at machine precision.

Combined, (a) and (b) verify the *local* (per-cell) discrete Noether identity that previous tests only verified in *integrated* form.

## 2026-05-16 (1/√6 origin — 2D-square test resolves the candidate question)

### New module: `ca_maxwell_2d.py` (composite-photon bilinear on the 2D Paper 1 Eq. 16 QCA)

Built to discriminate between three hypotheses for the leading curl-residual constant $1/\sqrt 6$ measured on the BCC lattice (`findings.md` Finding 7):

- Candidate A — neighbour-pair counting: $1/\sqrt{C(z,2)}$ with $z=4$ → predicts $1/\sqrt 6 = 0.408$ on **both** BCC and 2D-square (both have $z=4$ neighbours).
- Candidate B — dimensionality $\times$ bilinear norm: $1/\sqrt{2d}$ → predicts $1/\sqrt 6 = 0.408$ on BCC ($d=3$) and $1/2 = 0.500$ on 2D-square ($d=2$).
- Candidate C — tetrahedral half-angle: $\cos(\theta_\text{tet}/2)/\sqrt 2$ → predicts $1/\sqrt 6$ on BCC; not applicable on 2D-square.

`ca_maxwell_2d.py` ports the BCC composite-photon construction (`weyl_eigenmodes_*`, `bilinear_G`, `EM_bilinears`, `maxwell_curl_residual_*`) to 2D-square. The 2D Weyl unitary has a nonzero $\sigma_z$ component ($n_z = s_x s_y$), so the bilinear $G^i = \psi^T \sigma^i \psi$ is still a 3-vector and the cross-product structure of the Maxwell curl equation is unchanged. Only the lattice and the $n(k/2)$ profile differ.

**Measured 2D-square curl/k over $|k| \in \{10^{-5},10^{-4},10^{-3},10^{-2}\}$:**

| $\|k\|$ | curl/k (2D) | $\Delta$ vs $1/2$ |
|---|---|---|
| $10^{-5}$ | $0.5000000000$ | $+3.2\times 10^{-11}$ |
| $10^{-4}$ | $0.5000000000$ | $-4.7\times 10^{-11}$ |
| $10^{-3}$ | $0.4999999896$ | $-1.04\times 10^{-8}$ |
| $10^{-2}$ | $0.4999989580$ | $-1.04\times 10^{-6}$ |

Resolution: **curl/k → 1/2 to 10 decimal places**, 22% off the Candidate A prediction. **Candidate A is falsified; Candidate B is confirmed.** The constant is $1/\sqrt{2d}$ where $d$ is the lattice dimensionality, not a neighbour-pair-counting quantity.

The BCC measurement's apparent match to Candidate A was a numerical coincidence: $C(4,2) = 6 = 2\cdot d$ when $d=3$, and the regular tetrahedron's half-angle cosine is $1/\sqrt 3 = c_\text{lat,3D}$. None of these are causal — moving to $d=2$ with the same $z=4$ moves the constant cleanly to $1/2$.

**Subleading coefficient also dimensionality-driven:**

- 3D BCC: $\text{curl}/k = 1/\sqrt 6 + 0.01883\,k + \mathcal O(k^2)$ (linear-in-k correction)
- 2D square: $\text{curl}/k = 1/2 - 0.0104\,k^2 + \mathcal O(k^3)$ (quadratic-in-k correction)

The k-power of the correction mirrors the lattice dispersion's k-power: BCC has an $\mathcal O(k)$ dispersion correction along $(1,1,1)$ (Paper 4 Eq. 23); 2D arccos has only $\mathcal O(k^2)$ corrections in all directions. So the curl-correction starts at $k$ in 3D BCC and at $k^2$ in 2D square. Algebraic origins of $0.01883$ and $-0.0104$ are open.

**Sanity checks on 2D-square pass:**
- Dispersion residual: $4.71\times 10^{-9}$ at $k=10^{-3}$, scales as $\mathcal O(k^2)$.
- Transversality: $5\times 10^{-19}$ to $1.8\times 10^{-17}$ — machine zero.

**Exactness inventory updated** with new exact-algebraic line: *Composite-photon curl-residual leading coefficient = $1/\sqrt{2d}$*. Validated on $d=2$ (2D-square) to 10 decimals and $d=3$ (BCC) to 7 figures. New lattice tests (FCC, 4D hyperdiamond, 2D triangular) would add data points but are not currently scoped.

### Files updated
- `ca-simulation/ca_maxwell_2d.py` — new (~170 lines).
- `findings.md` — Finding 7 gets a "Resolution" section with the measurement table and verdict.
- `docs/theory/ca-reference.md` — exactness inventory entry promoted from open question to closed-form $1/\sqrt{2d}$.
- `docs/status/changelog.md` — this entry.

## 2026-05-16 (10× test execution pass)

### Fresh runs at 10× scaled parameters — data and matches to existing formulas

Per CLAUDE.md preference for exact equations vs machine-precision results, the 10× lattice/parameter bumps applied earlier today (see "10× lattice-resolution bump across the test suite") were executed for the tests that fit in memory, plus a 10× scan of the L3 curl residual in $|k|$ and a 10× bump on the D1 zitterbewegung step count. Outcomes:

**L1 — BCC unitarity, dispersion, small-k Weyl regression.** Survives the 10× bump bit-for-bit:
- L1.a unitarity: $\max |u^2+|n|^2 - 1| = 6.34\times 10^{-16}$ both helicities.
- L1.b $A_0 = I$ at $k=0$: exact ($U_+[0]=U_-[0]=1.0$).
- L1.c analytic vs measured dispersion: $7.22\times 10^{-16}$.
- L1.e small-k Weyl regression at $|k|=0.005$: $4.96\times 10^{-4}$ (unchanged; residual is a property of k, not L).
- L1.d norm drift scanned at $L\in\{40,80,120\}$, 200 steps: 8.6e-14, 4.7e-14, 6.2e-14 — all at the FFT round-off floor. Full $L=160$ run requires ~5 min wall time per direction; not completed in the available budget but the trend is established.

**L2 — Exact-arccos 2D Weyl.** Norm drift at L=320, n=200: $7.64\times 10^{-14}$; at n=2000 (extra 10× on steps): $7.63\times 10^{-13}$. Ratio = $9.985$ — **exact 10× per-step linear scaling**, confirming per-step drift = 1 ulp of complex128 with no algorithmic growth.

**L3 — composite-photon curl-residual 10× k-scan.** $|k| \in \{10^{-5},10^{-4},10^{-3},10^{-2}\}$. The curl/k constant converges to $1/\sqrt 6 = 0.408248290\ldots$ to 7 significant figures at $k=10^{-5}$, with leading correction $\Delta \approx 1.883\times 10^{-2}\cdot k$. The constant $1/\sqrt 6$ is now the **exact algebraic leading coefficient** of the BCC pointwise-bilinear Maxwell-curl residual (Findings 2 update).

**L4 — EMQG modified Poisson.** L4.a rel err: $1.39\%$ at L=640, σ=30 (prior $2.75\%$ at L=64, σ=3 with the same σ/L ratio) — modest 2× improvement from the finer k-grid. L4.b vacuum c=c_0 exact. L4.c at L=1280 requires ~10 GB Cayley LU; not executed.

**Phase D1 — Dirac CA.**
- Weyl regression at m=0, L=320: $1.55\times 10^{-15}$ (prior L=32: $5.10\times 10^{-16}$; 3× looser, consistent with $\sqrt{N_\text{cells}}$ scaling).
- Norm drift 1000 steps, m=0.3, L=320: $3.98\times 10^{-13}$ (prior L=32: $3.42\times 10^{-14}$; 11.6× looser).
- Dispersion residual at L=320: $1.28\times 10^{-17}$ (machine zero; slightly tighter than prior $8.88\times 10^{-17}$).
- **Zitterbewegung at 10× n_steps (5000→50000), L=48 σ=10**: rel err drops 3.53% → **0.026%**, FFT bin drops 5×. 135× improvement in error from 10× more steps confirms prior 3.53% was FFT-bin-limited, not physical (Findings 6).

**Phase B1 — group velocity at L=640, σ=80.** speed_ratio at k0=(0.3,0): 0.99946; at k0=(0.6,0): 0.99987. Prior L=128 σ=8: 0.92–0.98. Wider packet sharpens the centroid measurement; $v_g = c\hat k$ holds tighter at 10× σ.

**Phase E1 — Aharonov-Bohm at L=640.** Phase pickup err = $4.44\times 10^{-16}$ (identical to prior; eigenvalue-phase floor). |overlap| = 1.0 to 12 decimals. Norm drift with A0: $4.29\times 10^{-10}$ (prior $3.58\times 10^{-12}$; 120× looser, consistent with $\sqrt N$ FFT roundoff over 100 steps).

**Phase E2 — SU(2) parity at L=320.** measured left_e_pop = 0.35528551 vs analytic 0.35528551 — 8 decimals. Right leakage = 0.0 exact.

**Phase F1 — vacuum regression at L=320: DIVERGES with default n_phi_sub=1, PASSES with n_phi_sub=2.** New finding documented in `findings.md` Finding 4. The KG velocity-Verlet stepper is CFL-bounded: $dt_\text{sub} < 2/\sqrt{8+2\mu^2} \approx 0.667$ in 2D with $\mu^2 = 0.5$. With `dt=1.0, n_phi_sub=1` the effective sub-step violates the bound; at L=32 the noise doesn't grow fast enough to show, but at L=320 the dense spectrum diverges within 100 steps. With `n_phi_sub=2` (dt_sub=0.5), $\|\Phi-v\|=1.44\times 10^{-15}$, fermion diff $=2.16\times 10^{-15}$ — F1 passes at machine precision. Empirical critical dt at L=320 lies between 0.85 and 0.95 (CFL is the safe lower bound).

**Phase F2 — Higgs+Goldstone at L=640.** Higgs radial residual: $1.00\times 10^{-3}$ (prior $1.06\times 10^{-3}$; O(dt²) Verlet error, not L-limited). **Goldstone residual scales as exactly machine epsilon times $|k|$**: residual/(|k|·ε) ≤ 0.88 for every mode — promotes Goldstone-massless from "0.04% precision" to **exact algebraic result** (`findings.md` Finding 3). New entry to the exactness inventory.

**Phase F3 — symplectic Yukawa back-reaction at L=320 (sub-scaled, n_phi_sub=2).** $\|\Phi-v\| \in [0.59, 0.74]$ in 200 steps (prior L=64: 0.66–0.73). Total energy drift $H_\text{rel} = 0.0002\%$. Symplectic contract holds at 10× lattice.

**Phase F4 — symmetry restored at L=320.** $|\Phi|_\text{max} = 0.0$ exact, η match diff = $2.33\times 10^{-15}$, χ max = $0.0$. Bit-for-bit identity test survives the bump.

**Not executed (sandbox memory ~3.4 GB):** F3b at L=960 (Cayley LU ≈ 5 GB), L4.c lensing at L=1280 (≈ 10 GB), C1 Cayley arm at L=1280, L1.d 3D L=160 full run.

### Match to existing formulas

The "match to a known formula or data point" results from the 10× variation:

| Result | Existing formula matched | Match quality |
|---|---|---|
| Curl residual / k → $1/\sqrt 6$ | $1/\sqrt 6 = 0.408248290\ldots$ (BCC geometry constant) | 7 significant figures at $k=10^{-5}$ |
| Goldstone residual ≤ $|k|\cdot\varepsilon$ | Goldstone theorem (exactly massless) | sub-ulp per |k| at L=640 |
| D1 / L2 norm-drift / step ≈ $\varepsilon_\text{double}$ | FFT round-off floor (standard error analysis) | 1 ulp/step, linear in n |
| F1 stability boundary at $dt_\text{sub} \approx 2/\sqrt{8+2\mu^2}$ | Explicit-Verlet CFL on 5-point Laplacian | empirical critical dt is the safe upper bound; CFL is a conservative lower bound |
| Zitterbewegung freq → $2mc^2$ at 10× steps | Dirac equation prediction | 0.026% at 50000 steps; converges as FFT bin width allows |

No data matched an imaginary-number approximation in the sense suggested by the prompt example. All matches that emerged are real-valued algebraic constants ($1/\sqrt 6$, $\varepsilon_\text{double}$, the CFL bound, $2mc^2$).

## 2026-05-15 (v2 layered build)

### L1–L4 v2 layered build implemented and tested
- **New files:** `ca-simulation/ca_bcc.py` (L1, ~210 lines), `ca-simulation/ca_core_exact.py` (L2, ~150 lines), `ca-simulation/ca_maxwell.py` (L3, ~220 lines), `ca-simulation/ca_emqg.py` (L4, ~165 lines), `tests/runners/run_L_tests.py` (~210 lines, gates between layers).
- **What:** the four-layer v2 stack from `docs/theory/ca-unified-v2.md` landed end-to-end in one session, with explicit pass gates between layers.

#### L1 — BCC + exact arccos dispersion (Paper 1 Eq. 15)
- Implemented as a Fourier-space diagonal unitary `U(k) = u(k)·I − iσ·ñ(k)` per Paper 1 Eq. 15. One CA tick = one application of `U`; norm is conserved by construction (FFT round-off floor only).
- **Discovery:** the formula transcribed in `qca-papers-1-4-overview.md` line 53 had a sign typo on the second term of `ñ_y`. Direct verification: `u² + |n|² = 0.47` off unity at finite k with the transcribed sign; `4.4e-16` off with the corrected sign `ñ_y^± = ∓ c_x s_y c_z + s_x c_y s_z`. Code carries the fix with an inline note; reference doc still needs back-fixing.
- **Tests:** unitarity 7.9e-16 across BZ; A_0=I exact at k=0 (Paper 2's V7 constraint); analytic ω=measured eigenvalue phase to 7.2e-16; norm drift 3.7e-14 over 200 steps on a 16³ random spinor; small-k Weyl regression rel err 5e-4 at |k|=0.005 (scales as O(|k|²)).

#### L2 — Exact arccos in 2D (Paper 1 Eq. 16)
- `ca_core_exact.py::weyl_step_2d_arccos_splitstep` — the 2D square-lattice analog. Coexists with the existing linearized `weyl_step_2d_splitstep`; the new module is opt-in. 2D form is unitary by inspection: u² + |n|² = c_x²c_y² + s_x²c_y² + c_x²s_y² + s_x²s_y² = (c_x²+s_x²)(c_y²+s_y²) = 1 — no typo to fix.
- **Tests:** unitarity 3.2e-16 across BZ, A_0=I at k=0, norm drift 8.4e-15 over 200 steps on 32×32. **Frequency-dependent c measured:** Δc/c = −1.13% at |k|=0.5 along the (1,1) diagonal, machine zero along (1,0) — confirms Paper 4 Eq. 23's anisotropic prediction (V5 test in the literature reference).

#### L3 — Composite photon (Paper 1 Eq. 35)
- `ca_maxwell.py::weyl_eigenmodes_3d_bcc` extracts the two energy eigenmodes of `U(k)` at a chosen k by direct diagonalisation. `bilinear_G(psi, phi) = φ^T σ ψ` builds the σ^i bilinears (note: `φ^T`, transpose-not-conjugate, per the De Broglie photon construction). `EM_bilinears` computes `E_G = |n|(G_T + G_T†)`, `B_G = i|n|(G_T† − G_T)` after projecting out the longitudinal-to-2n̂ component.
- **Tests passing:** composite-photon dispersion `Ω_γ = 2ω(k/2) → |k|/√3` to 0.21% at |k|=0.05; transversality `2ñ·E_G = 2ñ·B_G = 0` to 4.6e-17.
- **Anisotropy verified analytically:** along (1,0,0) the dispersion is exactly `Ω_γ = k/√3` (residual 3.8e-15 — machine zero); along (1,1,1) the leading correction is `k/18` from the `sin·sin·sin` term in u(k/2), giving rel err 2.79e-3 at k=0.05. Both match the closed-form expansion.
- **Curl-equation residual is INFO only.** Paper 1 Eq. 35's `∂_t E_G = i·2ñ × B_G` holds for the *smeared* photon construction (Paper 1 lines 84-90, smearing function f_k(q)). For the pointwise bilinear used here, the curl residual scales as O(k) rather than the O(k³) expected for the smeared form — 2% at k=0.05, 0.04% at k=0.001. Documented as a known limitation; the full smeared-photon test is deferred research.

#### L4 — EMQG modified Poisson + c(φ) (Paper 6 Eq. 19.7)
- `ca_emqg.py::solve_poisson_2d` solves `∇²φ = 4πGρ` on the periodic lattice via FFT (`φ(k) = −4πGρ(k)/|k|²`, gauge `φ(k=0) = 0`). Static only; time-dependent retarded Poisson is future work.
- `c_field_from_phi` builds the position-dependent light speed. **Sign correction vs the v2 proposition:** the doc had `c = c_0/(1 + φ/c_0²)`, which with φ<0 in a well gives c>c_0 — light speeds up in the gravity well, opposite to GR lensing direction. Corrected to the GR-effective-medium form `c = c_0/(1 − 2φ/c_0²)`, which gives c<c_0 in the well and the right deflection direction. At |φ| ≪ c_0² this is `c ≈ c_0(1 + 2φ/c_0²)`, the leading-order Shapiro form, giving 4GM/(bc²) deflection.
- **Tests passing:** static Poisson recovers `∇²φ = 4πGρ` to 2.75% at L=64; vacuum ρ=0 → c=c_0 exactly (0.0 residual); **lensing demo** — Weyl probe at impact parameter b=18 deflects 0.6 cells toward a mass at the lattice centre, and doubling the mass gives 1.83× the deflection (8.5% off the expected 2.0 — within tolerance for a 128×128 grid at finite Δt).

#### Fresh full-suite regression (2026-05-15)
- `run_phase_tests.py`: **8/8 PASS**. All residuals identical to the 2026-05-14 baseline. D1 dispersion 8.88e-17, E1 phase pickup 4.44e-16, E2 right leakage = 0.0.
- `run_phaseF_tests.py`: **5/5 PASS**. All residuals identical. F1 fermion diff 8.41e-16, F2 dispersion 0.106%, F3 bounded |Φ−v| ≤ 0.73, F3b deflection −6.6 cells with norm drift 1.1e-15, F4 η match 7.57e-16.
- `run_L_tests.py`: **L1 6/6, L2 5/5, L3 3/3 + 1 info, L4 3/3 — all four layers PASS.**
- **Total across all three runners: 30/30 hard tests pass, 0 fail, 1 info note (L3 curl).**

#### What this *does* and *doesn't* establish
- ✓ The BCC lattice + exact arccos dispersion is a working substrate. The Paper 1 / Paper 2 small-k Weyl regression holds at machine precision; the lattice corrections at higher |k| are the published Paper 4 Eq. 23 ones.
- ✓ Frequency-dependent c is now an *observed* lattice property in the test suite, not just a theoretical claim.
- ✓ A photon-like composite object with the correct dispersion ω = |k|/√3 and transverse polarization exists on the BCC lattice as a Weyl-pair bilinear.
- ✓ A static Poisson solver coupled to the existing Cayley variable-c stepper produces qualitative Newtonian-style lensing with correct sign and approximately-linear mass-scaling.
- ✗ The composite photon's full Maxwell curl-equation closure is *not* established at the published O(k³) bound; only the dispersion and transversality pieces are. The smeared-photon construction needed for the curl is research-grade work, deferred.
- ✗ The Poisson solver is static; time-dependent retarded gravity (Paper 6 Eq. 19.6 / 19.7 with the ∂_t² term) is not implemented. F3b's static depression demo and L4's static-mass lensing are consistent in that they both ignore the retardation term.
- ✗ The L4 lensing scaling 1.83 vs 2.0 (8.5% error) is good enough to confirm direction and approximate magnitude, but not precise enough to claim a published Newtonian Δθ = 2GM/bc² match — that would need a higher-resolution lattice and a more careful integration of the photon trajectory.

## 2026-05-15 (latest)

### Drafted unification proposition v2
- **Files:** `docs/theory/ca-unified-v2.md` (new); `docs/status/project-status.md` (progress table row + Next-Steps bullet); this changelog entry.
- **What v2 changes from v1:** v1 (`ca-unified-proposition.md`) had one scalar Φ doing two jobs — Yukawa fermion mass *and* metric coupling $c \propto |\Phi|^{-\alpha}$. v2 keeps the v1 per-cell phase / Strang architecture and the Higgs Yukawa unchanged, but routes the metric coupling through a second field: the EMQG vacuum potential $\phi$ that solves Paper 6 Eq. 19.7 ($\nabla^2\phi - c_0^{-2}\partial_t^2\phi = 4\pi G\rho_{\text{tot}}$) with $\rho_{\text{tot}}$ summing Φ stress-energy and fermion stress-energy. Then $c(\mathbf{x}) = c_0(1 + \phi/c_0^2)^{-1}$ feeds the existing Cayley variable-$c$ stepper.
- **Four-layer stack:** L1 BCC substrate + exact arccos dispersion (Papers 1, 2); L2 Weyl/Dirac with exact $\omega_\mathbf{k}$ option (Papers 1, 4); L3 composite-photon U(1) (Paper 1 Eq. 35) + existing SU(2) and Φ; L4 EMQG modified Poisson sourcing $c(\mathbf{x})$ (Paper 6).
- **Why this is an improvement over v1:** (i) the v1 free parameter $\alpha$ is replaced by the measured Newton constant $G$; (ii) the v1 sign inconsistency between the published $c \propto |\Phi|^{-\alpha}$ and the F3b lensing demo (which needed the flipped exponent) goes away — the EMQG sign is unambiguous; (iii) fermion density couples directly to $\phi$ rather than only indirectly through Φ shifting first; (iv) closes the largest v1 architectural gap by adding a genuine composite-photon Maxwell sector (current v1 has only an external classical $A_\mu$).
- **Preservation contract:** every existing passing test (8 Phase A–E + 5 Phase F including F3b) is the gate for a specific limit of v2 (Φ=v, $\rho_{\text{tot}}=0$, $|\mathbf{k}|\to 0$, photon coherence enforced by hand). Nothing breaks; new tests are gained.
- **Build sequence recommended:** V4 composite photon (Paper 1 Eq. 35; new `ca_maxwell.py`, 4–6 days); V11 EMQG modified Poisson + Cayley $c(\phi)$ (1 week); V12 gravitational redshift from linear $c(z)$ (2–3 days); V6 BCC 3D substrate (1–2 weeks); V1/V5 exact arccos in 2D (2–3 days); V2 Klein paradox (2 days); V3/V7 audits (½ day).
- **Caveats called out:** EMQG is a physics essay, not a derivation (Paper 6 motivates Eq. 19.7 from the Fizeau analog, not from informational axioms); the time-dependent lattice Poisson solver is new engineering and is the largest implementation risk; composite-photon coherence under interactions is non-trivial; WEP violation at $10^{-40}$ remains unfalsifiable in float64.

## 2026-05-16 (later)

### 10× lattice-resolution bump across the test suite

Every test runner had its lattice-spacing parameters scaled up by a factor of ten in each spatial dimension. The intent is to expose any artifact that has been hiding under coarse discretization, and to give the v2-build L3/L4 sectors a finer substrate to verify against.

**What was changed.** Linear lattice sizes `L` and proportionally-scaled spatial-feature parameters (Gaussian widths `σ`, impact parameters `b`, depression widths `σ_phi`, packet starting offsets) were all multiplied by 10. Step counts that index *time* resolution were either left unchanged (where the test measures a frequency or phase that the FFT bin already resolves) or scaled with `L` (where the test measures a path length).

| File | Test | Old | New | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run_L_tests.py` | L1.d (3D BCC norm drift) | $L=16$ | $L=160$ | $\sim 4.1\text{M}$ cells; FFT working memory $\sim 250\,\text{MB}$ per spinor component. |
| `run_L_tests.py` | L1.f (3D BCC single step) | $L=12$ | $L=120$ | Trivial. |
| `run_L_tests.py` | L2.e (2D exact-arccos norm drift) | $L=32$ | $L=320$ | Trivial. |
| `ca_emqg.py` | L4.a (test_point_source_potential) | $L=64$, $\sigma=3$ | $L=640$, $\sigma=30$ | 2D Poisson, source width scales with L. |
| `ca_emqg.py` | L4.b (test_vacuum_c_uniform) | $L=32$ | $L=320$ | Trivial. |
| `ca_emqg.py` | L4.c (test_lensing_deflection) | $L=128$, $b=18$, $\sigma=6$ | $L=1280$, $b=180$, $\sigma=60$ | Cayley LU memory at L=1280 is $\sim 10\,\text{GB}$ (O(L³) for 2D nested-dissection). Fallback to $L=384$ or Krylov solver if RAM-bound. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | A1 (Bloch coloring) | $L=32$ | $L=320$ | Visualization only. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | A2 (visualization frames) | $L=64$, $N_1=96$, $\sigma=5$, snap=240 | $L=640$, $N_1=960$, $\sigma=50$, snap=2400 | Bump on the propagation arms; 8×8 graph plot kept (display scale, not physics). |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | B1 (group velocity) | $L=128$, $n_{\text{steps}}=60$, $\sigma=8$ | $L=1280$, $n_{\text{steps}}=600$, $\sigma=80$ | FFT propagator. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | B2 (size sweeps) | $L\in\{8\dots64\}$, $\sigma\in\{0.5\dots5\}$, $L_\sigma=32$ | $L\in\{80\dots640\}$, $\sigma\in\{5\dots50\}$, $L_\sigma=320$ | Pass criterion bumped (`L≥320`, `σ≥30`). Log-x axis added so the plot stays readable. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | C1 (refraction, 3 arms) | $L=128$, $\sigma=8$, $n_{\text{steps}}=200$ | $L=1280$, $\sigma=80$, $n_{\text{steps}}=2000$ | Cayley arm has the same LU memory caveat as L4.c. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | D1 (Dirac CA: Weyl regression, norm, dispersion, zitterbewegung) | $L\in\{32, 96\}$, $\sigma\in\{3, 14\}$ | $L\in\{320, 960\}$, $\sigma\in\{30, 140\}$ | Zitterbewegung `n_steps=5000` and `dt=0.5` are *time* params unchanged. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | E1 (Aharonov–Bohm) | $L=64$, $\sigma=8$ | $L=640$, $\sigma=80$ | Flux $\pi$ is a topological invariant — unchanged. |
| `run_phase_tests.py` | E2 (parity violation) | $L=32$, $\sigma=4$ | $L=320$, $\sigma=40$ | `c=0` disables propagation; `n_steps=63` measures isospin angle only. |
| `run_phaseF_tests.py` | F1 (vacuum regression) | $L=32$, $\sigma=3$ | $L=320$, $\sigma=30$ | Bit-for-bit identity test; should still hit machine precision. |
| `run_phaseF_tests.py` | F2 (Higgs/Goldstone dispersion) | $L=64$ | $L=640$ | dt parameters unchanged. |
| `run_phaseF_tests.py` | F3 (Yukawa back-reaction) | $L=64$, $\sigma=5$ | $L=640$, $\sigma=50$ | dt, n_steps, μ², λ, y all unchanged. |
| `run_phaseF_tests.py` | F3b (Cayley gravity demo) | $L=96$, $\sigma_\phi=12$, $\sigma_{\text{pk}}=8$, $\Delta y=18$, $n_{\text{steps}}=120$ | $L=960$, $\sigma_\phi=120$, $\sigma_{\text{pk}}=80$, $\Delta y=180$, $n_{\text{steps}}=1200$ | Pass threshold on `deflection` rescaled from −0.05 cells to −0.5 cells. |
| `run_phaseF_tests.py` | F4 (symmetry restored) | $L=32$, $\sigma=3$ | $L=320$, $\sigma=30$ | Bit-for-bit identity test. |

**What is *not* changed.** Brillouin-zone *sampling density* in L1.a / L2.a (the $K = \text{linspace}(\ldots, 16, \ldots)$ k-grids used for unitarity sweeps) is left at 16 per axis — that is a verification-fineness, not a lattice resolution. Dimensionless wavevectors (`k_in=(0.5, 0.15)`, `k0=0.5`, flux $\pi$) are unchanged. Mexican-hat parameters $\mu^2, \lambda$, Yukawa $y$, Newton-analog $G$, c values, dt and n_steps for time-domain frequency tests are all unchanged.

**Tests not yet re-run at the new size.** The runners are updated but a regression pass at the new sizes has not yet been executed. The most likely failure mode is the Cayley arm of C1 and the L4.c lensing test running out of RAM on the LU factorization; the runners now carry inline notes pointing to feasible fallbacks ($L=384$–$512$) if that happens. Bit-for-bit regression tests (F1, F4, D1 Weyl-regression-at-m=0) should still hit machine precision because they are pure equality checks on identical update rules at a larger array shape.

## 2026-05-16

### P1 — symplectic Yukawa back-reaction (corrected c² factor)
- **Files:** `ca-simulation/ca_unified.py` (`unified_step` gains `back_react=True` Strang-symmetric half-kicks; new `total_energy` helper); `tests/runners/run_phaseF_tests.py::test_F3` rewritten to use the symplectic API and a 200-step run with a bounded-energy check.
- **Derivation:** the Yukawa Hamiltonian density is `H_Y = c²·y·(Φ·η†χ + Φ*·χ†η)`. The factor `c²` is essential because the Dirac stepper uses `m·c²·β` for the mass-Hamiltonian — without it, the Π update is too small to balance the Ψ rotation, leaving a constant Strang offset (1.85% drift independent of dt). With `c²` included, Hamilton's equation `δΠ/δt = -∂H_Y/∂Φ* = -c²·y·χ†η` matches the Ψ rotation generator and the symplectic split is exact.
- **Test result:** `max|H − H₀| / |H₀|` over 200 steps with dt=0.5 is **3 ppm** (was 1.85% before the correction). Drift scales as O(dt²) exactly: dt=0.5 → 3.0e-6, dt=0.25 → 7.4e-7, dt=0.125 → 1.8e-7, dt=0.0625 → 4.6e-8 (every factor-2 dt drop reduces drift by 4×).
- **|Φ−v| range** dropped from 0.012–1.247 (old divergent sketch, 30 steps) to 0.66–0.73 (200 steps), confirming the back-reaction stays in a bounded oscillation around vacuum instead of running away. F1/F2/F4 unchanged with the default `back_react=False`.

### P3 — Cayley/Crank–Nicolson exact-unitary variable-c kinetic step
- **Files:** `ca-simulation/ca_curved.py` (added `_build_cayley_matrix_2d`, `CayleyVarcSolver2D`, `weyl_step_2d_varc_cayley`, `method='cayley'` option in `measure_refraction`); `tests/runners/run_phase_tests.py::test_C1` extended with a Cayley arm; new `test_F3b` Newtonian-gravity demo in `run_phaseF_tests.py`.
- **What:** solves `(I + i·dt/2·H_disc)·ψ_new = (I − i·dt/2·H_disc)·ψ_old` where `H_disc` is the Hermitized variable-c Weyl operator with face-averaged c (`c_face(i+½,j) = (c(i,j)+c(i+1,j))/2`). Sparse 10-nonzero/row matrix; LU-factored once per c-field change via `scipy.sparse.linalg.splu`. Sub-stepping (`n_sub`) inside the solver reduces O(dt²) Crank–Nicolson dispersion without giving up exact unitarity.
- **Norm conservation:** drift 5.5e-15 over 200 steps on variable c (range 0.2–0.6) vs **32.6%** for the existing Strang-split stepper. That's a 6×10¹³ improvement — Cayley is at the complex128 machine-precision floor.
- **C1 refraction:** Cayley measures the right qualitative refraction direction but is 5.4° off the continuum Snell prediction at |k|≈0.5. This is the **lattice dispersion** of centered first-differences (ω(k)=c·sin(k) instead of c·|k|), not a bug. The existing Strang+FFT path avoids it for the c₀ baseline because the FFT propagator gives exact-k dispersion. Closing the gap requires a higher-order spatial stencil — flagged as future work.
- **F3b — gravitational lensing demo:** static |Φ| depression at the lattice centre (sigma=12, depth 0.35); `c(x) = c₀·(|Φ|/v)^α` with α=1.5 so the depression makes c *slower* at the centre (the proposition's published sign `(-α)` is inconsistent with F3's depression direction; the `+α` form is what produces attraction). A Weyl probe packet at impact parameter +18 cells is deflected an extra 6.6 cells **toward** the depression vs the flat-c baseline. Norm drift 1.1e-15.
- **Test totals:** 13/13 phases pass (A1–E2 + F1–F4 + F3b). Full Phase A–E suite still 8/8 unchanged.

Phys.* 45, 1137); `2 - Simple Derivation of the Weyl and Dirac Quantum CA.pdf` (Raynal 2017, arXiv:1703.05890v2); `3 - Special Relativity Derived from Cellular Automata Theory.pdf` (Ostoma & Trushyk 1999); `4 - Weyl Dirac and Maxwell Quantum CA.pdf` (Bisio/D'Ariano/Perinotti/Tosini 2016, arXiv:1601.04842).
- **Why:** Papers 1, 2, 4 together establish the rigorous QCA framework (Cayley graph from informational principles → BCC lattice in 3D / square in 2D → Weyl, Dirac, Maxwell in the small-wavevector limit) that the project's `ca_core.py`/`ca_dirac.py` implementations are *closely related to but not identical to*. The overview maps the agreements (machine-precision Weyl/Dirac dispersion, Zitterbewegung at 2mc², per-cell U(1) phase structure), divergences (our 3D code uses a simple-cubic lattice; Papers 1–2 prove BCC is the unique non-trivial 3D choice; our split-step uses linearized $\omega = c|\mathbf{k}|$, the QCAs use the exact $\arccos$ dispersion), and absent components (composite-photon Maxwell construction not implemented; mass-parameter $n^2+m^2=1$ constraint not enforced). Paper 3 is qualitatively different — physically motivated rather than formally derived — and contributes the mass-vs-force reinterpretation that is observationally indistinguishable from standard SR.
- **Tests proposed:** V1 exact QCA dispersion (group velocity at non-trivial $|\mathbf{k}|$ matches $\nabla_{\mathbf{k}}\arccos(c_x c_y)$); V2 Klein paradox plateau matching Paper 4 Fig. 3; V3 $n^2+m^2=1$ constraint audit; **V4 composite photon construction** (biggest physics value — implement Paper 1 Eq. 35 as a Maxwell sector derived from Weyl bilinears); V5 frequency-dependent $c(\mathbf{k}) \approx 1 \pm k/\sqrt 3$; V6 BCC vs simple-cubic in 3D; V7 $A_0=0$ audit (Paper 2's central proof); V8 deformed-Lorentz (DSR) signature; V9 cosmic-ray spreading numeric spot-check; V10 mass-as-force-decrease is undecidable, no test arises.

### Added structured summary of Ostoma & Trushyk (1999) *Cellular Automata Theory*

- **Why:** the paper sits in the same lineage as Fredkin's *Digital Mechanics* (already in the project) and is the most CA-physics-aligned external source in the workspace. Captured the postulates, equations, and falsifiable predictions in math-mode markdown per project conventions.

### P4 — replaced `m_eff = y·|Φ|` with `m_eff = y·Re(Φ)`
- **File:** `ca-simulation/ca_unified.py::unified_step`.
- **Why:** `|Φ|` is non-analytic at Φ=0 (the F4 vacuum). For the F1 vacuum at `Φ = v` real, `Re(Φ) = |Φ| = v`, so F1 regression is preserved at machine precision (`|Φ−v|=1.11e-16`, `max fermion diff=8.41e-16`). For F2/F3 Φ stays close to real so Re(Φ) and |Φ| agree numerically; F2 dispersion residuals unchanged (max 1.06e-3 radial, 4.42e-4 Goldstone); F3 sketch range `max|Φ−v|=6.97e-01` vs 6.95e-01 previously (within sketch noise). F4 unchanged at exact zero.
- **Tests:** `run_phaseF_tests.py` → 4/4 pass.

### P2 — complex Yukawa bilinear
- **Files:** `ca-simulation/ca_dirac.py` (added `_mix_eta_chi_complex` and `dirac_step_2d_varm_complex_splitstep`), `ca-simulation/ca_unified.py::unified_step` (rewired to pass `Re(Φ)` and `Im(Φ)` separately).
- **What:** the Standard-Model Yukawa Lagrangian `L_Y = -y·(Φ·η†χ + Φ*·χ†η)` produces a *complex* mass `M(x) = y·Φ(x)` coupling η→χ with `y·Φ` and χ→η with `y·Φ*`. The new per-cell rotation `exp(-i·c²·dt·[[0, M·I],[M*·I, 0]])` is exactly unitary; eigenvalues are `±|M|·c²·dt` with eigenvectors `(1, ±M*/|M|)/√2`. Handles `|M|=0` via the well-defined limit `sin(θ)/|M| → factor`.
- **Strang structure:** kinetic step at the real baseline `m_0 = mean(m_R)` (existing `dirac_step_2d_splitstep`), per-cell complex δ-rotation for `(M−m_0)`. Reduces exactly to `dirac_step_2d_varm_splitstep` when `m_I ≡ 0` (verified bit-for-bit, `diff = 0.000e+00`).
- **Unit tests added inline (one-shot):**
  1. `m_I=0` reduction → 0.0 difference vs real stepper.
  2. `m_I≠0` norm conservation over 500 steps → drift 6.8e-14.
  3. Sensitivity: toggling `m_I` from 0 to 0.5 changes the output by 8e-2 after one step (proves the code path is exercised).
- **Tests:** Phase F suite 4/4 pass; full Phase A–E suite still 8/8. **Total 12/12.**
- **F1 regression preserved** because vacuum Φ=v is real → m_I≡0 → complex rotation reduces to identity-after-subtraction → kinetic step matches reference Dirac CA at constant `m = y·v` bit-for-bit.

## 2026-07-20 - 14:30 — F257: Bethe logarithm from the model's own hydrogen spectrum (Lamb shift now fully model-derived)
Removed the last literature input in the F252 Lamb shift — the tabulated Bethe logarithm — by computing ln k0(n,l) from the model Hamiltonian's Coulomb resolvent (`ca-simulation/ca_bethe_log.py`). A direct pseudostate sum converges hopelessly slowly (the log weight loads onto high-energy virtual states; length/velocity/acceleration gauges all crawl), so the state-sum is turned into a one-dimensional t-integral over the Coulomb Green's function via ln(e)=∫[1/(1+t)−1/(e+t)]dt (Dalgarno-Lewis): ln k0 = (1/D)∫[M3/(1+t)−M2+tM1−t²M0+t³F(t)]dt + ln2, with energy moments M_p=⟨g_r|(H_l−E)^p|g_r⟩ (length source g_r=r·u_n) and the resolvent F(t)=⟨g_r|(H_l−E+t)^{-1}|g_r⟩ a stable tridiagonal solve of the inhomogeneous radial equation — i.e. it samples the model's own spectrum through its resolvent. Below-threshold/degenerate intermediate states (for 2p: 1s below, 2s degenerate) are projected out of the resolvent and added explicitly. On a uniform grid with O(h) Richardson the method reproduces ln k0(1s)=2.920, ln k0(2s)=2.748, ln k0(2p)=−0.037 vs accepted 2.984/2.812/−0.030 (~2-3%, no literature constant); the residual is a continuum/short-distance representation systematic (M3 is continuum-dominated), removable with a log grid or Sturmian basis (noted). Feeding the model-derived ln k0 back into the F252 self-energy (+ the F251-derived Uehling −27.1 MHz) gives a FULLY MODEL-DERIVED Lamb shift of 1059.9 MHz vs measured 1057.845 MHz (0.2%; the literature-Bethe calc gives 1052.2, both within ~0.6% — the slight overshoot is partly fortuitous compensation of the ~2% low ln k0(2s) against omitted higher-order QED). The F252 Lamb shift is now free of any tabulated atomic-QED constant. Seven phases P0–P6 with acceptance gates, critical path P0 → P1 → P3.1. The BCC photon port is gated behind a derivation spike, since a degraded even-law photon would be a worse outcome than a split lattice. Roadmap fact-checked against the tree by a verification pass; 24 citation and count errors corrected before commit. No code changed.

## 2026-07-29 - 20:10 — Visualisation seam: `field_dump` observer + dependency-free VTK writer, and a kernel-coverage addendum to §P6

Built the dump/render seam so the engine never imports a renderer: `src/casim/io/vtk.py` is a hand-rolled VTK XML writer (ImageData `.vti` with appended raw little-endian binary + UInt64 headers, plus `.pvd` time-series collections) and `FieldDump` in `src/casim/engine/observers.py` drives it — `{type: field_dump, every: N, format: vti|npz|both, stride: S, components: bool, precision: float32|float64, max_mb: B}`. One `.vti` per channel per tick plus a per-channel `.pvd` that opens a whole run as a ParaView animation; ParaView, PyVista, napari, and numpy all read the output with **no new dependency on the physics path** (a `.vti` is a short XML header plus raw bytes, ~100 lines to write). Two failure modes are guarded because both produce plausible *wrong* pictures rather than errors: (i) VTK point order is x-fastest (`p = ix + nx*(iy + ny*iz)`) against numpy's C order, so every array is raveled `order="F"` and `tests/casim/test_field_dump_vtk.py` asserts the index formula longhand — the test cannot pass by agreeing with the writer's own ravel; (ii) the writer **refuses complex arrays** and the observer splits them into explicitly named `f_re`/`f_im`/`g_re`/`g_im` volumes, so no renderer can silently drop Im(ψ) (CLAUDE.md's chiral-transform caution). Channels with no spatial volume (compute-once spectral solves, the 2ⁿ many-body register) are skipped and listed in the summary rather than failing a run. Verified end-to-end: `bcc_weyl`, `photon_pair` (components mode), `gravity_deflection`, and all 7 channels of `unified_hydrogen` dump; `njl_pion` and `entanglement_register` skip cleanly; 9/9 tests pass (run through a standalone harness — the sandbox could not install pytest, so the pytest invocation is unverified and should be run natively). Also wrote `docs/design/visualization.md` recording the decision (offline-first via the dump; keep vispy for live stepping with the per-frame `np.percentile`-sort/`np.abs`-copy/realloc costs fixed; add a matplotlib slice viewer as the cheap default debug view; pyqtgraph/vedo/PyVista rejected as *live* renderers) and the honest scope note that ~every claim this project makes is spectral or algebraic, so 3-D rendering is a debugging and intuition instrument, not a measurement one. Separately, `docs/audits/2026-07-29-kernel-coverage-addendum.md` re-derives the kernel-coverage gap independently and lands on the same **67 of 106** as §P6 (corroboration), then adds four sectors the §P6 table omits (matter binding/bound states — `ca_atom`, `ca_element`, `ca_baryon_dynamics`, `ca_baryon_blockspin`, `ca_nuclear_core`, `ca_photon_bs`; dynamical processes — `ca_emission`; applied physics — `ca_superconductivity`, `ca_slowlight`; plus `ca_rotation`, `ca_ir_coupling`, `ca_ir_bremsstrahlung`, `ca_qstar_logmoment`, `ca_eg_sextic_coupling`, `ca_induced_stiffness`, `ca_propagator`, `ca_vacuum_energy`, `ca_casimir_materials`), one correction (**`ca_link_hamiltonian` is not compute-once** — `evolve_krylov`/`evolve_dense` are real-time evolution and F110's purpose was to leave frozen-link Euclidean measurement; it is the only genuinely tickable unwired kernel and would be the engine's first real-time confinement channel, since `gauge_mc` is Euclidean MC on frozen links), two tombstone candidates (**`ca_unified`** implements `deprecated/ca-unified-proposition.md` and is superseded by CLAUDE.md decision 3's Higgs-free hypercharge, yet its 6 tests run unmarked in the battery — recommend adding it as a sixth entry to §P6's superseded-families list; **`ca_casimir_materials`** is the only kernel of 106 with zero tests and no `main()`), and a concrete resolution for the reachability criterion §P1.4 is asked to settle (`ca_cooling` executes during a `casim run` via `forks/lgt_fork_A_mc` while being invisible to a direct-reference scan, so the three candidate criteria give 67 / 66 / ≥67 — recommend measuring "driven by a registered channel or compute-once wrapper", the least flattering, and reporting the transitive closure separately). No physics changed; no kernel moved or edited.

## 2026-07-29 - 14:20 — F265: gauge action migrated from composite-SC to genuine BCC plaquettes

An audit flagged the gauge channels as cubic and matter as BCC. The split turned out to run between **propagator and action**, not gauge and matter: every gauge propagator (γ, W, Z, gluon) already evaluated `ca_bcc.bcc_dispersion(k/2,±)`, while every gauge action built field strength from three ⟨100⟩-plane square plaquettes over straight length-2 composites of BCC link pairs (`# Build 6 effective SC links from BCC pairs`, `a2 = 4.0`). F265 proves that construction has an **exact kernel**: setting the three composites to the identity leaves one of the four ⟨111⟩ link axes completely free, so `S_SC ≡ 0` to round-off on configurations where the genuine BCC Wilson density is ≈1, at *every* amplitude including perturbative. A rank count gives the exact sizes — per colour component `rank_BCC = 3N_s−3` vs `rank_SC = 2N_s−4`, blind directions `N_s+1`, i.e. asymptotically 1/4 of the raw link space and **1/3 of the curvature-carrying content**. The defect was undetectable by normalisation checks because both actions have the *identical* classical continuum limit (both `→ 8 F_μν F^μν`, difference identically zero in sympy), so the migration moves neither the continuum limit nor `g_lat`. New module `ca_bcc_gauge.py` implements the BCC minimal loop — the 4-bond rhombus, 6 orientations modulo translation, area 2√2, ⟨110⟩ normals, single O_h orbit — with the closed-form 6→3 reconstruction `f = (1/8) Σ_p m_p Φ_p` (exact because `Σ_p m_p m_pᵀ = 4·I`). `ca_wmu.plaquette_field_strength` and `ca_gluon.plaquette_field_strength_su3_bcc` now delegate to it with unchanged signatures and dict keys; the superseded bodies are kept as `_*_composite_sc` for re-demonstrating the no-go only. `test_wmu_phase3` (5/5) and `test_FG7_gluon_dynamics` (20/20) pass unchanged. Also closes the open roadmap item on the composite plaquette's Bianchi residual with a negative answer: the composite converges at O((ka)³) rather than the generic O((ka)²) because its three planes share one simple-cubic sublattice where the cube-face product is an exact lattice identity — that anomalously good behaviour *is* the restriction to 3 of 6 orientations, so no transform should remove the cross-direction terms. Test `tests/findings/test_F265_bcc_gauge_geometry.py`, 12/12.

## 2026-07-29 - 14:20 — `ca_lattice.py` becomes the single source of truth for BCC real-space geometry

Previously *all* BCC-ness in the repo was injected by the momentum-space `ca_bcc._bcc_uvec` and `ca_lattice.py` had no BCC helper at all (only `cubic()` and `square()` constructors). Added a BCC geometry section: `BCC_HOP_DIRS` (8 body diagonals), `BCC_LINK_AXES` (4 independent axes), `BCC_PLAQUETTES`/`BCC_PLAQ_NORMALS`/`BCC_PLAQ_LABELS`/`BCC_PLAQ_AREA` (the 6 rhombic orientations), `BCC_INDEX_IN_Z3`, `BCC_BZ_COPIES`, `bcc_site_mask`, `bcc_reciprocal_basis`, `bcc_bz_volume`, `bcc_bz_mask`, `make_kgrid_bcc`. Two facts now enforced in code rather than assumed: with the conventional cubic cell at side 2 the lattice is `{n : all components same parity}`, index 4 in ℤ³, so **only 1/4 of a cubic array's sites are lattice sites**; and the reciprocal lattice is **fcc**, `V_BZ = 2π³ = (2π)³/4`, so the cube `[−π,π)³` holds exactly **4 copies** of the true BZ (independently reproducing F264 row 326 from the hop set rather than the Weyl-point census). `bcc_bz_mask` selects exactly `N/4` grid points for every even `L` by coset reduction, and the three fcc shifts are verified to be genuine periods of the integer-hop walk (1.1e-15). This makes the `ca_bgfield_loop` BZ measure error explicitly fixable — it integrates a BCC propagator over the cubic BZ with a mod-2π wrap, over-counting the spatial measure by 4 — but the correction is **not** applied or re-run in this session; it remains open along with the 4D hypercubic `ca_lpt_*` chain, `ca_hypercharge` (still 2D despite its docstring), and the F94/F146 Monte-Carlo actions.

## 2026-07-30 - 08:52 — Roadmap P0 executed: constants provenance, supersession ledger, and the repo's first gate

Executed all five steps of Phase P0 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-unified-program.md`); overview in `docs/status/P0-completion-overview.md`. No physics changed — P0 buys the ability to *notice* when it does. **P0.1** built `src/casim/constants/` (five sector modules, 20 constants). Per decision D2 the registry owns provenance, not values: which finding fixed each constant, its exactness class, its derivation, and every site that re-defines it. Constants that legitimately disagree are registered **separately** rather than reconciled — `f_pi` is three constants (92.07 model anchor / 92.4 PDG target / 92.28 Γ-convention), `sin²θ_W` is two (F45 UV cap 1/4, F49 on-shell 2/9, reconciled by F231), and `cos3δ*` is two (derived cos(2/3)=0.7858873 vs empirical F93-O7 0.785874, whose 1.7e-5 gap *is* the F256 near-coincidence); merging any of these would launder a prediction into an input or erase the model's own caveat. `alpha_eff_star` is stored as the bracket [0.376, 0.411] with no value, since the quoted 0.39 is a midpoint of two scale choices, not a determination. Fixed in passing: `a/ℓ_P` now resolves to the exact closed form √(8π)·3^(1/4) rather than the circulating 5-sf `6.5978` (3.4e-6 relative), with truncated sites held to their own precision; and `GeV_per_lattice_unit`, the lattice↔MeV bridge that existed only as a comment, is now computable. **P0.2** added `tests/casim/test_constants_consistency.py` — 34 assertions by AST inspection, never import (importing a findings test executes its physics and writes JSON), plus sweeps for `c_lat` (~74 definitions, two spellings) and any fourth `f_pi`. The sweep immediately found two definitions the audit missed, both legitimate and now allowlisted with reasons: `derive_velocity_addition.py` at 1/√2 (2-D square lattice) and `forks/curl_fork_cubic.py` at 1.0 (simple-cubic fork, where the emergent light speed is the quantity under test). **P0.3** extracted `docs/theory/supersessions.yaml` — 7 machine-readable records from the 9 `key-decisions.md` prose bullets, each carrying both sides plus a `retained:` field, since almost nothing here is superseded wholesale. **P0.4 changed on contact with the repo:** the inherited audit claim that ~14 test files should be marked superseded was wrong — exactly ONE is (`test_F114_dielectric_black_hole.py`); eleven are partial (a dead verdict around live algebra: F62's lapse-mix *sign* is production code, F52's factor-2 discriminator is still canonical under F178, F179's 3δ*=Q check is now the model's falsification handle), one is a deliberate historical baseline, and several were keyword false positives — `test_F91_pairing_classification.py`, flagged as superseded, *is* the supersession authority. Blanket-marking those twelve would have silently retired working coverage while looking tidy, so the unit of supersession is a **check**, not a file: one `@pytest.mark.superseded` (with `addopts = "-m 'not superseded'"`), and docstring banners naming DEAD vs STILL LIVE checks for the rest, generated idempotently from the ledger by `tools/apply_supersession_banners.py` (banners rather than markers because 166 of 272 findings tests have no `def test_` at all). Also resolved the live contradiction in `derive_generator_norm_from_F118.py` — it now carries a PRE-DECISION FRAMING banner: its Schur-isotropy R=1 proof is the F255 content and stands, and its circularity argument is real and is precisely *why* the F234 arrow was reversed, so it is recorded rather than deleted. **P0.5** stood up the first automated enforcement in the project's history: `tools/run_gate.py` + `Makefile` + `.github/workflows/gate.yml`, 11 checks in 7.9 s, which asserts rather than scraping stdout and refuses to pass silently when pytest is absent (it runs standalone and announces reduced mode). The CI workflow ends by perturbing `G_LATTICE` and *requiring* the gate to fail. Acceptance verified against the live tree with four negative tests (perturbed constant, stale banner, removed banner, orphan banner) — all exit 1 — then restored; `git diff` confirms the 13 test-file edits are purely additive. Regenerated indexes.

## 2026-07-30 - 10:05 — P2.1 (pyfftw) + Roadmap P1: the test suite can now fail

Executed P2.1 and all of Phase P1 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-unified-program.md`); overview in `docs/status/P1-completion-overview.md`. Gate is now 15 checks in 9.6 s. **P2.1** — `ca_fft` has preferred pyfftw since it was written and exposed `set_workers` for it, but pyfftw was never a declared dependency, so every run in this project's history silently used single-threaded scipy. Wiring it up exposed a real bug: the six pyfftw call sites never passed `threads=`, so even with pyfftw installed the FFTW path would have run single-threaded — `set_workers` was documented for it and did nothing. Fixed, plus a 60 s plan-cache keepalive (without it every transform re-plans, costing more than threading gains). Declared as the `fast` EXTRA rather than a hard dependency, because pyfftw needs the FFTW C library and a platform wheel and a failed build would block `pip install -e .` entirely — turning a performance option into an availability risk; CI asserts the backend really is pyfftw so a missing wheel fails loudly. The fallback is no longer silent: `casim backend [--bench]` reports and benchmarks the active library and every `make gate` prints it. New `tests/casim/test_fft_backend_equivalence.py` holds the contract every future backend (incl. the P2.4 GPU one) must meet: agreement to 1e-12 relative, round-trip identity, and thread count cannot change results — the specific regression risk introduced by wiring `threads=` through. **P1.2, the core of the phase** — 232 of 344 test files have no `assert` and ~70 had no failure mode by any mechanism. The roadmap proposed a new `tests/baselines/` store; on inspection that was unnecessary, because those tests already write their numbers to `test-results/*.json` and those files are already committed, so HEAD *is* the baseline — no duplicate store to drift, and accepting a change uses the workflow that already exists (read the diff, `git add` deliberately). Built `src/casim/baselines.py`: numeric-only comparison (volatile keys filtered by regex after the first run flagged `total_elapsed_s` as physics drift), relative tolerance with an absolute floor so FFT-floor wobble is noise, structural vs value changes reported separately. Wired into `runner.run_battery_script` so any script rewriting a result artifact is diffed against HEAD, with **drift outranking a printed PASS** — a script's own gates can be satisfied while a physical value moves underneath. Verified end-to-end: perturbing `B_F95` −5.69e-2→−5.72e-2 turns `test_F234_Wvc_triple_closed.py` (6 PASS tokens, exit 0) into FAIL naming `C_req_from_delta_and_B 0.036201→0.036392`. Added a third outcome: a script that reproduces its committed numbers exactly is now a genuine PASS with the reason stated; only tests with nothing to diff remain `RAN`, and the detail text says so. **P1.4** — `test-results/manifest.json`, bidirectional result↔test↔finding. Two bugs found: the finding-ID regex `\b(F[A-Z]?\d{2,3})\b` never matched `F107_...` because `_` is a word character so there is no trailing boundary (that one character is why the old index resolved almost nothing by finding ID and guessed from filename prefixes); and a naive finding-ID join yields 825 links because every test merely mentioning F107 matches every F107 result — a mapping saying a result belongs to nine tests is not a mapping. Evidence is now ranked (declared-source → exact-stem → stem-prefix → declared-finding → finding-ID), stops at the first matching tier, and accepts finding-ID only when unique; the rest are recorded as AMBIGUOUS rather than wired up wrongly. Also exploited the `finding` key present in 117 result JSONs. Links 1280→268 (237 exact-stem), tests resolved 234→241, orphans 158→116, ambiguous 30. **P1.1** — three tiers: gate (`tests/casim`), battery (findings+priority+**runners**+scenarios), archive (ledger `fully_superseded` only, so partially-superseded files keep running). `tests/runners` (45 files) was in an opt-in group absent from `DEFAULT_GROUPS` and had never run; battery is now 343 files (122 pytest, 221 scripts), 1 archived. **P1.3** — 320 of 344 files execute physics at import, so `--collect-only` runs the suite. Restructuring all of them is long, risky, and should not precede the baselines P1.2 just created, so this delivers the instrument and a RATCHET instead: `tools/audit_tests.py --ratchet` fails if unfalsifiable (70), import_time_work (320) or no_assert (232) regresses; verified by adding a throwaway test, which trips all three. The numbers can now only go down. **P1.5** — full generation is not yet honest: the class vocabulary across 391 result JSONs is not standardised (`exact` 61, `machine-precision` 34, `machine` 31, `quantitative` 132, plus `Tier-B`/`PREDICTION`/`structural` and channel classes like `coupled`/`background` that are not exactness classes), and only ~69 files record a residual, so a generated table would look confident with silent holes. `tools/gen_exactness_inventory.py` therefore emits the 147 derivable rows into a marked section of the single inventory file and states its coverage (147 classifiable of 530 residual entries; **383 unclassified**), leaving the hand tables authoritative and retiring the disconnected casim-scoped split. Its staleness check produced the phase's sharpest number: the hand-maintained inventory header reads F140 / 2026-06-11 against a newest finding of **F265 — 125 findings behind**. **Acceptance:** three of four criteria met; the fourth (zero tests in `RAN`) is PARTIAL and reported as such — the mechanism exists and the 241 tests with artifacts have left limbo, but ~70 emit nothing at all and need per-file assertions with judgement about what each claims. Regenerated indexes and manifest. One note for Ben: the drift checker flagged numeric movement in pre-existing uncommitted work (`wmu_phase3.json` slope 0.8%, `ym_action` 20%) — left untouched, but a 20% Yang–Mills action shift is a physics change worth reviewing before commit.

Wrote `docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`, the companion arc to `roadmap-unified-program.md` (whose P0 and P1 are complete). Where the P-roadmap built the instruments — provenance, a supersession ledger, a suite that can fail — this one uses them to collapse the two-layer structure into one program. **Six decisions taken, one of them a reversal.** D6 supersedes **D2**: the 124 `ca-simulation/ca_*.py` kernels and 47 forks migrate into `src/casim/engine/` by sector and `ca-simulation/` is deleted. D2 bought safety at the cost of a permanent seam; P1's baseline engine now supplies that safety directly, so every move is proved bit-identical against committed result artifacts and the seam has no remaining justification. D7 inverts the constants registry from provenance-only to value-owning — the mitigation P0 itself named — which flips `test_constants_consistency.py` from *"every recorded site agrees"* to *"no unregistered literal exists"*, and makes the 12 currently-unenforceable sites enforceable because the pattern they hid behind (function default arguments) disappears when the value comes from an import. D8 makes `casim.numerics` the only numerics surface (no module imports numpy/scipy/`ca_fft` directly), which is the same problem as B6's decorative performance seam solved once instead of twice — so P2.2/P2.3/P2.4 fold into phase C1. D9 makes tests declarative registry entries with four kinds and two result contracts (assertion → direct pass/fail; result_dump → baseline diff vs HEAD), run only through `casim test`, with `--param k=v` making parameter sweeps first-class; a `legacy_script` kind gives 100% registry coverage on day one so the 345-file migration becomes a falling counter rather than a big bang, and P1.3's import-time-work problem dies structurally because a record names an *entry function*. D10 (user decision) merges cleanup with migration: the original is copied to `deprecated/code/` as backup and the cleaned copy lands at its new engine path, one touch per file. D11 gives every subpackage a module registry (sector, findings, exactness, reachability, tests), which is what makes C8's `casim index` generate `code-index`/`tests-index`/`findings-index` from declared data instead of filesystem scraping — retiring the prefix heuristic that leaves 104 of 339 rows empty — and what turns P6's "67 of 106 unreachable kernels" from a survey into a queryable field. Ten phases, each written as a standalone Claude session brief (entry state, read-first list, deliverables, acceptance gate, handoff): C0 instrumentation (import graph, dead-code *proposer*, the 171-file migration manifest, an atomic `migrate_module.py` that rolls back on any drift), C1 numerics façade, C2 constants inversion, C3 engine skeleton + BCC base layer, C4–C6 sector migrations (gauge / particles / interactions), C7 test registry, C8 `casim index` + full exactness inventory, C9 retirement of `ca-simulation/`. Critical path C0 → C3 → C6 → C7; the P-roadmap's P3 (one lattice, one clock) is unblocked by C3 and P6 dissolves into C6+C8. The two highest risks are both cleanup risks, and both are mitigated by P0's own lesson that of 14 files an audit called superseded exactly one was superseded wholesale: C0.2 *proposes* dead code and a human accepts it per file, and `derive_generator_norm_from_F118.py` is named in the plan as the pattern to follow — migrate with a banner rather than delete, because its Schur-isotropy proof is the F255 content and its circularity argument is *why* the F234 arrow was reversed. No physics changed; no file moved.

Two pre-existing bugs fixed in passing, both surfaced because C0 made the gate run in a second environment: `test_resume_bit_identical(tmp_path="/tmp")` — pytest does not inject a fixture for a parameter carrying a default, so the default silently defeated the `tmp_path` fixture and every run in the project's history wrote its checkpoint to a shared world-writable `/tmp` under a fixed name; and `run_gate.py` wrote scenario smoke output to fixed `/tmp/gate_<name>.json`, so two users or sessions on one machine collide and the second gets a PermissionError from the gate rather than from anything real. Both now use private temp dirs. Regenerated indexes.

## 2026-07-30 - 12:10 — Roadmap C1 (partial): the numerics façade is built and enforced; C1.3/C1.4 stopped on a live concurrent session

Executed C1.1, C1.2, C1.5 and the code half of C1.6 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`); overview in `docs/status/C1-completion-overview.md`. **C1.3 and C1.4 were deliberately NOT attempted** — see the stop reason at the end. **C1.1** built `src/casim/numerics/` (`xp`, `fft`, `linalg`, `rng`, `chiral`, `backends`) and the point of it is the *unification*: this repo had TWO seams for the same job and neither was load-bearing — `ca_fft` selected numpy/scipy/pyfftw with a module-level string and an `if/elif` in every wrapper (a library seam every kernel used), while `casim.lattice.backend` held an object registry with a seven-method protocol (a device seam that **zero physics modules imported**; its only importers were two tests and its own self-registration). A GPU backend registered in the second would never have been reached by a kernel calling the first. `casim.numerics.backends` is now the single registry with the library choices as backend *objects*, so `use("pyfftw")` and `use("cupy")` are the same kind of statement. `ca_fft.py` was migrated with C0's tool (backup in `deprecated/code/`, shim at the old path until C9) and `casim.lattice.backend` became a shim too. New surface: `rfftn`/`irfftn` (the real-valued E and B fields were paying full complex transforms), `batched_matmul` (BLAS `zgemm`; `np.einsum` does not dispatch batched products to BLAS), per-consumer seeded RNG streams so adding a channel no longer perturbs every other channel's numbers, and the explicit-real chiral primitives. `xp` is deliberately honest — it IS numpy today, because genuinely swapping the array namespace would change the type every `ca_*` module receives and every `isinstance` check in the tree; what C1 makes swappable is the transform layer, and the docstring says exactly that rather than implying more. MLX is registered but **cannot auto-select**: it is float32-backed (eps 1.2e-7 against the 1e-12 gate), so it requires `CASIM_ALLOW_FLOAT32=1` and a test asserts no device backend activates on its own. **C1.2** extended the equivalence contract with five checks — `rfftn` equals the `fftn` half-spectrum and round-trips (a wrong substitution still broadcasts and its norms still look plausible while the high half of k-space is gone), `su2_apply` preserves both components and is norm-preserving, `components_preserved` is itself tested against a deliberately dropped imaginary part, and no device backend auto-selects. **One claim was written and then retracted**: the first draft of `casim.numerics.linalg` said `batched_matmul` matched `np.einsum` "to the last bit". Measured, it does not — BLAS sums in a different order, giving ~9e-16 absolute / ~4e-16 relative (1–2 ULP), and `optimize=True` does not close it. That is four orders below `MACHINE_FLOOR` so it cannot move any gate the model asserts, but a call site cannot be swapped and then called bit-identical; per the roadmap's own risk row the tolerance is recorded and the test asserts a bound, not equality. **C1.5** added `tools/audit_numerics.py --ratchet` (219 physics modules scanned, 168 still importing numpy/scipy/ca_fft directly — numpy 167, ca_fft 17, scipy 7), wired into `make gate` and verified by adding one `import scipy.linalg` to a physics module, which trips it. **Three defects found and fixed along the way, none of them C1's own.** (i) The `ca_fft` migration showed "drift" in 3 of 13 baselines; re-running against the UN-migrated `ca_fft` produced identical deltas, proving it environmental — the committed baselines were made with scipy's FFT and this machine has only numpy.fft, exactly the risk the roadmap names. That produced two real `casim.baselines` fixes: `wall_seconds` was being reported as physics drift because the volatile-key regex had `wall(_time|_s|_clock)?` but not `_seconds` (the same false-positive class P1 fixed for `total_elapsed_s`), and a relative test at the round-off floor is meaningless — 2.1e-14 → 2.9e-14 is a 27% relative change and pure noise — so `MACHINE_FLOOR = 1e-12`, quoted from `EXACTNESS_CLASSES`, now classifies a pair with BOTH values below it as `kind="floor"` rather than `"changed"` (default off, floor deltas still returned and counted, never dropped). (ii) **C0's shim template would have broken every later migration**: it imported the target without putting `src/` on `sys.path`, and 12+ test files do `sys.path.insert(0, "ca-simulation")` and import a kernel with no reference to `src/` anywhere. Every one would have started failing with `ModuleNotFoundError: casim` the moment its kernel moved. The shim now walks up to find `src/casim` itself; verified with `PYTHONPATH` unset. (iii) The module graph's scipy count was inflated 86× — the regex matched a bare `sp.`, which in this repo is `sympy` in 63 files, reporting 1,124 scipy sites against 13 real ones; C1 sizes its work from those numbers so an inflated one is worse than an absent one. **Stop reason.** At 12:07 a scan showed ~25 kernel files under `ca-simulation/` and seven under `src/casim/` modified within 40 minutes, including a new `src/casim/constants/measured.py` — C2.3's `MeasuredConstant` escape hatch verbatim from this roadmap. A parallel session is executing C2, confirmed with Ben. C1.3's migration list (`ca_colour_dielectric`, `ca_gluon_self_energy`, `ca_maxwell`, `ca_photon_pair`, `ca_wmu`, `poisson_open`, `ca_multigrid`) is precisely what C2 is rewriting, and migrating a file another agent is mid-edit on would copy a half-edited file into `deprecated/code/` as "the pre-clean original" — the one artifact in this design that has to be trustworthy. So C1.3/C1.4 are deferred rather than attempted; this is the concurrent-session collision the risk table names (F110, F129, F262, now this) and the prescribed mitigation, claiming the sector first, is what was applied. Gate is 10 of 15 passing; none of the five failures is a C1 defect — two are C2's in-flight constants work (`A_OVER_LP`/`A_OVER_LPLANCK` truncations that P0's overview claimed were held to their own precision via a `Site.expected` override which is in fact `None`, verified pre-existing by reproducing it with every C1 change stashed) and three are derived indexes stale from both sessions adding files, which whoever finishes second should regenerate. Not measurable on this machine and reported as such rather than claimed: the ≥3× wall-clock target (scipy and pyfftw would not install over a throttled link) and a second backend clearing the contract (no MLX or CuPy on a Linux sandbox).

## 2026-07-30 - 17:24 — Roadmap C2 executed: the constants registry now owns values, and the consistency test asks the opposite question

Executed all five steps of Phase C2 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`); overview in `docs/status/C2-completion-overview.md`. **C2.1** inverted the registry per decision D7 — `casim.constants` owns values, not just provenance, and every registry symbol is now an importable Python name (`from casim.constants import c_lat, G_LATTICE, delta_star`), with the exported name *being* the registry symbol so there is no second naming scheme to keep in sync. Exact constants resolve from closed form and never a decimal; rational constants export the `Fraction` under the symbol and a float under `<symbol>_f`, so `3 * delta_star == Fraction(2, 3)` is an exact comparison rather than 0.6666…, while array code takes `delta_star_f`. **C2.1b** deleted the literals: 174 substitutions across 42 files in `src/` and `ca-simulation/`, driven by an explicit table and an AST-span rewriter rather than by hand, and **34 of 34 substituted values are bit-identical to the literal they replaced** (verified programmatically, not asserted). `Site` gained a `kind` field — `import` / `literal` / `reexport` / `runtime` — so the registry records *how* each path binds a constant, and the test asserts that a site claiming `kind='import'` really does import it, which is the same class of check as P0's stale-path test one level up. **C2.4** flipped the consistency test's polarity from *"every recorded site agrees with the registry"* to *"no unregistered site exists"*, with the sweep engine in `tools/audit_constants.py` so the gate and the new `--ratchet` counter cannot diverge. Rogue literals in `src/` + `ca-simulation/`: **202 → 0**; `tests/` holds 277 and is C7's, counted and ratcheted but not yet enforced. The 12 sites P0 recorded as unenforceable are now enforceable, exactly as predicted: `gap_solve(..., Lam3=0.347)` was outside the static reader's reach only until the default named an imported symbol. **C2.3** replaced P0's five-entry `ALLOWLIST` dict with typed `MeasuredConstant` records in `casim/constants/measured.py`, in two kinds — `measured` (the same quantity in a regime where the answer genuinely differs: the 2-D square lattice at 1/√2, the simple-cubic fork at 1.0, and F79's own derivation of a/ℓ_P, which must not be checked against the registry's copy of the answer) and `coincidence` (a different quantity that happens to share the value). Ten records, each with a mandatory reason; a `MeasuredConstant` without one raises at import, which is the whole difference between this and an allowlist. Two of P0's five entries were **deleted rather than ported**: `spectral_matter.TARGETS` and `ca_chiral_anomaly.F_PI_MEV` needed an exemption only because a literal check could not tell them from a drifted anchor, and both now import the constant they actually mean. **C2.5** took the registry from 20 constants to 43, closing the QCD-calibration block the 2026-06-06 audit named as its largest cluster of underived inputs (Λ_NJL, GΛ², m₀, g_A, M_N, r_c, g_ρππ — registered *because* they are inputs, since an input no registry names is one that can quietly be re-described as a result), the SI/CODATA anchors, m_D/m_V, M(0)'s third unit system, and λ₆'s two inputs. **The sweep's own findings are the interesting part**, and all follow C2.2's rule that constants which coincide stay separate: the model carries **2/9 three times over** in three sectors for three unrelated reasons — the E_g representation weight (lepton, F175), the on-shell m_W²:m_Z² count (electroweak, F49/F141), and the colour-Fierz coefficient of the one-gluon-exchange→NJL scalar channel (strong, F77/F116, named "the Fierz 2/9" in F256), which accounted for most of the 42 flagged 0.2222s and now has an owner; **0.733 twice** (the F92/F118 lepton saturation amplitude and the F151/F155 q\* matching scale, which is 0.7327); **1/√3 twice as different kinds of object** (c_lat, a speed in cells per tick, and the F151 q\* band edge, a momentum scale in 1/a — `QSTAR_LO = c_lat` would typecheck and read as nonsense); and **ħ twice at different precisions**, where `ca_superconductivity` computes h/(2π) from the SI-exact h and substituting CODATA's 10-digit quote would have *quietly coarsened* it by 6e-11, so it is registered as `hbar_SI_from_h` and left computed. **Drift: two values moved, both anticipated by the roadmap's own risk row.** `A_OVER_LP` (6.59782, 6-sf) and `A_OVER_LPLANCK` (6.5978, 5-sf) now carry the exact √(8π)·3^(1/4) — relative moves of 5.06e-7 and 2.53e-6 — which shifts six numbers in `F233_mass_scale_N_transmutation.json` by 6.7e-8 to 9.6e-7 (α_s(M_Z) 1-loop 0.11954572, Λ-ratio 1.7766530). The truncation was the defect and F107 makes the closed form canonical, so this is accepted rather than tolerated; `F144_route_a_alpha_s.json` shares the input and could not be re-run here (no scipy on this machine) — **it needs re-running and its drift accepting before commit.** Perturbation blast radius is now countable per constant from the module graph: c_lat 271 modules / 141 named tests, f_pi_anchor 22/12, c_fierz_colour 12/6. **One cross-session defect found and fixed by causing it:** regenerating the migration manifest reset `ca_fft.py`'s `migrated:` stamp to null while the C1 session's migration was in fact complete, and `check_deprecated.py` then reported the tree INVALID — `gen_migration_manifest.py` now preserves `migrated`, `drift` and `dead_symbols` across regeneration (`_STATE_FIELDS`), and the stamp is restored with a note. C4–C6 explicitly invite parallel sessions, so a regenerator that clobbers migration state would have scaled badly. Gate gains `tools/audit_constants.py --ratchet` (rogue_gate 0, rogue_backlog 277, literal_sites 13 — all three only go down) and `make constants-report`. Not from this session and still open at hand-off: `tests/casim/test_backend.py` fails because its `__main__` calls a function C1 renamed mid-flight, and the wmu/gluon drift C1 already reported (including the 20% `ym_action` shift) is untouched.

None of it depends on the field being propagated, only on `(shape, sign, block)`. Measured at L=32: `weyl_step` 4.71 → 2.43 ms (1.94×), `chiral_rs_step` 13.78 → 7.94 ms (1.74×). The cache provably changes nothing — agreement with the audited kernels is identical before and after to every digit (`1.4043333874306805e-15`, `1.7763568394002505e-15`) — and cached arrays are marked read-only so a caller that mutates one gets a loud `ValueError` instead of silently corrupting every later tick; `tests/casim/test_chiral_core_caching.py` pins that, plus the requirement that four distinct geometries produce four distinct cache entries, since a key collision would serve one geometry's unitary to another. **A second false claim retracted.** `chiral_core` said it matched `ca_bcc.weyl_step_3d_bcc` and `ca_wmu.w_propagation_step_chiral` "bit-for-bit".
