The `".."` run was expanded 2→5 and nothing else, so each expression resolves to the same absolute directory as before; verified mechanically (5-up == repo root for all 19, no 2-runs left, all parse) and empirically (importing the forks wrote F228/F238 JSON at the repo root). Sibling `sys.path` self-inserts were deliberately left alone (fork families moved together) as were the four `OUT = dirname(__file__)` forks (unchanged semantics). `ca_vacuum_energy`'s `<my dir>/forks` injection became a package import of `casim.engine.forks.gravity.gr_fork_F164_cosmological_constant`; F192 re-runs 3/3 with identical physics. C5 independently hit the cwd-relative version of the same hazard and built `engine/particles/_results_path.py` — worth unifying at C7/C8. **Cleanups.** *F114:* resolved by **relabelling, not cutting** — `F114_enlargement_pct` is not a strippable symbol but a dict key inside the live `eht_predictions()`, `test_F186` T2 asserts on it (GR shadow < F114, enlargement pinned +4.63%), and it is a committed baseline key; so the docstring now states it is the standing **exclusion record** and never a prediction, the inline comment reads `EXCLUDED (F178)`, and the ledger note goes from `STALE … Flagged for P6` to `RESOLVED at roadmap C6` with reasons. Roadmap §8's "F114 stays checkable" is the point. *F50/F52/F62:* **nothing to strip** — `ca_gravity.py` carries only the canonical F64 dielectric, F106 T⁰⁰ sourcing and the retained (ledger-`protected`) F62 `lapse_mix_half`; the superseded two-leg mechanism lives only in the gravity forks, which are the falsification record. Recorded in the ledger so the next reader does not hunt. **Wiring:** 46 import statements in 27 `src/` files re-routed (alias always preserved); five **string-loaded** references fixed by hand since neither the rewriter nor the C3.4 checker can see a string — `fields/entanglement.py` (C4's handoff item), `fields/strong.py`'s last bare `ca_dual_gl_backreaction`, `entanglement_register._QN`, and both lazy loaders in `casim/gravity/__init__.py`, which mattered most because they resolve inside `try/except → None` and would have failed **silently** at C9. **Constants provenance repointed — 49 `Site`/`MeasuredConstant` paths**: a `Site(kind='import')` names the file that imports the constant, and post-migration that file is a shim importing nothing, so the registry described a migration that had un-happened. C2's gate caught it exactly as designed (15 unverified sites + 5 rogue literals whose path-matched exemptions stopped matching); after repointing, **rogue literals 5 → 0** and every remaining unverified site is C5's. **Registry (D11):** `engine_module_files()` now *walks* the sector tree (one-level listing saw none of the 46 forks while reporting full coverage); `Module` gains `status` from the manifest so fork standing is a query (24 `fork_live` / 23 `fork_unclaimed`, every fork carries one); and **`reachable_from` is finally populated** — `gen_module_graph.py` runs one closure per channel module and credits its channel *types* to that closure, so the graph says *which* channels reach a module. Both `reach` and `reachable_from` are the **union of a module's two nodes** (shim + target), because reading either alone makes a migrated module look less connected than before — `lgt_fork_A_mc` is the case: shim `test-only`, target `driven` from `engine/core/tier3.py`. **P6's kernel coverage is now a value, not a survey: 47 of 177 engine modules are channel-driven.** A ledger-integrity fix fell out of the repointing: `gen_migration_manifest.py` aliases target→source when indexing the ledger and dead-code signals, so citing a module's new path can no longer silently downgrade `partial`→`live` or drop `protected_symbols` (verified on `ca_gravity`). **Verification:** all 91 importable C6 targets import clean; **no C6 target contains a physics change** — every target's line-by-line diff vs HEAD is a C1 fft substitution, a C2 constants substitution, a C6 import re-route, a C6 depth comment, or the raytrace docstring, and nothing else (31/98 byte-identical to HEAD outright); `casim list-channels` unchanged (29 types); `make gate` green except four items, **none a C6 code change** — two name C5 files (`_results_path.py` unregistered, `derive_generator_norm.py` orphan banner), the 11 remaining unverified constant sites are all C5's, and `test_backend::test_ca_fft_alias…` is the pre-existing scipy-vs-numpy failure C2/C3/C4 all recorded. Artifact churn classified against the machine floor: 5 timing-only, 5 round-off-floor, 4 with real movement — `F233`/`wmu_phase3` (documented pre-existing) plus **`F144_route_a_alpha_s` at 6.7e-08, which is C2's not C6's** (`running_alpha_s`'s `A_OVER_LP` is now the exact closed form where HEAD had the 6-sf `6.59782` — the discrepancy C2's own gate predicted; deterministic across re-runs, so it is a baseline C2 changed and did not regenerate). **One red flag that is NOT C6's and needs attention: `test_FG3_quark_electroweak` is 3/6 against a 6/6 baseline** (QE1 cold-w, QE4 norm drift 0.99, QE6 colour charge) — structural, not round-off; a transitive-closure query over the module graph shows **no C6 module in its import closure** (it imports only `ca_dirac`, C5-migrated today, and `ca_strong`, C4). Regenerated graph, dead-code proposal, migration manifest, results manifest and all five indexes.

## 2026-07-30 - 21:40 — Roadmap C5 executed: the particles sector is migrated, and three "regressions" turn out to predate every C-phase

Executed Phase C5 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`); overview in `docs/status/C5-completion-overview.md`. Verified in FULL mode with the vendored scipy/pytest env. **Sector claimed first** (`claims.particles`, 20:59) — and it mattered, because a C6 session claimed `interactions`+`forks` three minutes later and was writing those trees concurrently, so the import-rewiring tool was given an ownership rule read from the manifest (inside `engine/particles/` repoint every migrated kernel; elsewhere repoint only imports *of* a particles kernel, and skip any directory claimed by another live session) — 23 import lines across 23 files, **zero writes into C6's trees**. **All 21 `sector: particles` records migrated** byte-faithfully via `migrate_module.py`'s own imported templates (`tools/_c5_migrate_particles.py`; the sandbox refuses `unlink`, so the journal cannot roll back — C4's wall, C4's workaround). **Zero dead symbols accepted, deliberately:** all nine proposals are genuinely unreferenced yet all nine are load-bearing record — `dirac_step_2d_varm_splitstep` has an **open F62 follow-up** and is cited by two live docstrings, `dirac_chi_diamagnetic` is a named object of F153's method section, `proton_colour_field`'s docstring *is* the ε_abc negative result — and `derive_generator_norm_from_F118.py` migrated with its PRE-DECISION FRAMING banner intact, its ledger `code:` path repointed so the banner stops being an orphan. **Three defects the move exposed:** the three lepton-shape derivations wrote to cwd-relative `"../test-results/"` (replaced by a lazily-resolved `_results_path.py`) **and wrote at import with no `__main__` guard**, so any recursive import — pytest collecting `src/`, `pkgutil`, `casim index` at C8 — would have silently overwritten a committed baseline that `make drift` compares; and `migrate_module.run_tests` **overwrote `PYTHONPATH` instead of prepending**, dropping the vendored pytest and reporting every pytest-only test as a *test failure* rather than a broken environment (fixed in the C0 tool — C6 and C7 depend on it). **Drift: C5 introduces none, proven two ways** — every target is SHA-identical to its original and the import rewrites are a semantic no-op by **object identity** (148 callables, `old.fn is new.fn`, because the shim re-exports by `globals().update`); and every one of the 14 artifacts that moved above the 1e-12 machine floor was attributed by re-running the same test against a pristine `git archive HEAD` extract (`tools/_c5_drift_triage.py`). That experiment **resolves the red flag C6's entry left pointing at this sector: `test_FG3_quark_electroweak` is 3/6 at HEAD too, reproducing the identical deltas to 1e-15 — its committed 6/6 baseline was already stale before any C-phase, as is `FG2`'s** (both are no-assert result-dump tests, so they exit 0 while their numbers move and nothing watches). Separately, `figures/T1`/`T5` integer observables (`pkt_ticks` 75270→75273, deterministic) do reproduce at HEAD, so that drift is real and belongs to **C1.3's FFT routing of `ca_higgs` (9 lines, pre-C5)** against C5's 2 provably-inert import lines — invisible to C1's gate because it diffed artifacts it never re-ran. 57 of 64 sector tests pass; 4 exceed the sandbox wall-clock cap and are recorded as **not run, not as passing**; the 3 failures are pre-existing path defects unmodified from HEAD (a function that never existed, an off-by-one `dirname`, a hardcoded foreign session mount path). Gate 16/18 — both reds are elsewhere: C6's live C3.4 check, and the pre-existing scipy-vs-numpy `test_backend` failure C2/C3/C4 each recorded. Registry 178/178, 15 `casim.constants` `Site()` paths repointed, ledger 19/0, indexes regenerated.

## 2026-07-30 - 19:30 — Roadmap C7 executed: tests are declarative registry entries, and 30 of P1's 70 unfalsifiable tests turn out to have been writing committed numbers all along

Executed Phase C7 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`, decision **D9**); overview in `docs/status/C7-completion-overview.md`. Verified in FULL mode with the vendored scipy/pytest env, alongside **two live concurrent sessions** (`nifty-great-volta`, whose C6 `interactions`/`forks` claim is still open, and `bold-sweet-turing`). **Sector claimed first** — `claims.tests` written into the migration manifest before anything was touched, stating explicitly that `tests` is *not* a module sector (it covers `tests/**`, `tests/registry/`, `src/casim/tests/`, `tools/audit_tests.py`, the pytest collector) and touches no `modules:` record; it survived a full manifest regeneration, and **no file inside another session's claim was written**. **What landed:** `src/casim/tests/{registry,runner}.py` plus `tests/registry/*.yaml` — **349 records covering 346 test files, 0 unregistered**: 82 `assertion`, 226 `result_dump`, 3 `scenario`, **38 `legacy_script`**; `evidence:` is generated and every other field human-owned and preserved across regeneration, exactly as `dead_symbols` is in the migration manifest. **The teeth are in `validate()`, not in a convention:** a record claiming `assertion`/`result_dump`/`scenario` while having no way to fail does not load, so a test is either falsifiable or *labelled* debt and there is no third state a record can express — the structural closure of P1's honest PARTIAL on "zero tests in RAN limbo". **The phase's real finding was not what the roadmap expected.** P1 measured 70 tests with no failure mode; 57 of them *do* write numbers into `test-results/`, and those files are *already committed*. The results manifest could not see it because its strongest signal is filename-stem identity, and these files write under unrelated names (`tests/priority/test_09_GR4_mercury.py` → `top10_T09_GR4_mercury.json`; `test_FG7d_baryon_singlet.py` → `FG10_baryon_singlet.json`). Reading the artifact path **out of the source** turns an inference into a declaration, and a declared committed artifact is a baseline diff — a real failure mode with no edit to the test. Two acceptance rules recorded per artifact in `evidence.artifact_rule` (`local` = a write call within 8 lines; `file` = the path is a module constant and the file writes something), and rule `file` is only safe because of the **mtime guard**: a run that does not actually rewrite an artifact the record names reports `SKIP` ("nothing was actually compared"), never `PASS` — without it a wrongly-declared baseline reads green forever, because the working-tree copy simply *is* the committed one (`su3-noether` is the live example). Declared debt **68 → 38**; `no_assert` 232 → 231; `import_time_work` 321 → 320. **Selectors (C7.2):** `--id/--finding/--sector/--kind/--tier/--exactness/--path/--param`, with the scale-tier suite untouched behind the same subcommand. `--param` works end to end: perturbing δ\*=2/9 → 0.2 on `F234-Wvc-triple-closed` flips the closure's own `all_pass` 1→0 and moves 15 numbers, while `--param delta_star=2/9` reports **no drift** (the exact-rational path round-trips) and **the committed baseline is verified untouched** — a sweep writes to a temp dir, because a tool that lets a perturbed run overwrite the accepted values quietly moves the project's numbers. Making that record sweepable removed **the third local redefinition of δ\*=2/9** (named in the roadmap's C5 scope): `test_F234_Wvc_triple_closed.py` now takes `delta_star`/`B_F95` from `casim.constants` as keyword arguments, and its unparametrised run reproduces its artifact exactly, so the refactor moved no number. **pytest delegates structurally (C7.3):** `pytest_ignore_collect` hides `tier: archive` records (replacing P0's `superseded` marker with a registry field) and hides entry-driven records from *file* collection so nothing runs twice under two contracts; `tests/casim/test_registry_integrity.py` asserts the converse — every collected file has a record, and the registry's `gate` tier **is** the default pytest scope — so "pytest ≡ `casim test --tier gate`" is enforced rather than re-checked (`pytest tests/casim` 121 passed/1 skipped; the 16 gate records reproduce the identical set). `run_gate.py`'s three hand-coded scenario smoke runs became `kind: scenario` records driven by `casim test --tier gate --kind scenario`, which also moves their tick counts to where every other test parameter lives; `casim test` exits 1 on an empty selection, so deleting the records cannot quietly stop the smoke test. **Retirement (C7.6): one file of 347** — `test_F114_dielectric_black_hole.py` → `deprecated/tests/`, ledger `path:` repointed (so `make supersessions` and the banner checker keep covering it) and its banner restamped. The ledger classifies 14 test files and exactly one is `fully_superseded`; the other thirteen stayed — eleven partially superseded (live algebra inside a dead verdict), one deliberate `historical_baseline`, and `test_F91_pairing_classification.py`, which *is* the supersession authority. **Two defects, one fixed:** `test_backend::test_ca_fft_alias_delegates_to_the_active_library` — the failure C2/C3/C4/C5/C6 each recorded as "pre-existing scipy-vs-numpy" — asserted *bit-identical* agreement with **numpy** on a repo where P2.1 made `pyfftw` installable and C1 routed the alias through `casim.numerics`; FFTW and numpy differ in the last bits by construction, so it tested "the alias resolves to numpy", the opposite of its own name. It now asserts bit-identity against `casim.numerics.fft` (exact — same object) plus agreement with numpy to the `machine` bound 1e-12, and **`pytest tests/casim` is green for the first time in five phases**. Second: the baseline diff had to adopt `floor=MACHINE_FLOOR`, because the first real `result_dump` run reported `S_even 4.44e-16 → 0.0` as drift — both sides below 1e-12 and therefore indistinguishable by the project's own `machine` class, the exact trap `casim.baselines` documents; sub-floor deltas are now **reported separately, never dropped**, `expect: {strict_floor: true}` opts back in, and the rewritten artifact was restored to HEAD rather than accepted. **Ratchet gains its fourth key** (`legacy_script`, at 38), and `unfalsifiable` deliberately keeps its manifest-derived definition at 70 rather than being redefined against the registry — relabelling it would make the number improve without any test improving; `gen_manifest.py`'s stem heuristic is scheduled for deletion at C8.1, where the drop becomes real. **Deviations recorded:** `sector` uses the module-sector vocabulary rather than the sketch's `lepton` (C8 joins the two registries; two meanings of "sector" breaks both queries — the physics grouping is `--finding`), records with no import evidence at all (96 self-contained sympy/numpy derivations) take their sector from **the sector of the modules that own their finding**, a D11 join; `expect: {gates: all}` added as an explicit sentinel because an empty `gates: []` gates nothing and validation rejected it on the first attempt; `DEBT` and `SWEEP` are status values, not variants of PASS. **Gate 17 of 18; drift: none introduced** — all 7 drifted artifacts predate the session (latest mtime 17:18 vs session start 17:49) and the three artifacts C7 rewrote are bit-identical to HEAD. The one red is `no src code imports a ca-simulation shim path (C3.4)` — 52 imports, 23 in `engine/interactions/` and 29 in `engine/forks/`, **52 at entry and 52 at exit**, in two sectors claimed by the still-open C6 session and therefore deliberately not touched. `import_time_work` is 320, not *below* 320: the structural fix exists (a record names a module and a function, so import-time physics is impossible for a migrated record) and converting the remaining ~300 files is the second C7 session's work, as P1 chose the ratchet over a rushed refactor. Regenerated module graph, dead-code proposal, migration manifest, results manifest, exactness inventory and all five indexes.

## 2026-07-31 - 06:15 — Roadmap C8 executed: CASIM owns the indexes, and the finding-number audit finds ten collisions where the roadmap knew of three

Executed Phase C8 (`docs/roadmaps/roadmap-casim-consolidation.md`); overview in `docs/status/C8-completion-overview.md`. Verified in FULL mode with the vendored scipy/pytest env. **Sector claimed first** — `claims.indexes` (C8, 05:21 UTC), stated as not a module sector (it covers `tools/regen_indexes.py`, `tools/gen_exactness_inventory.py`, `tools/gen_manifest.py`, `src/casim/index/`, the five `*-index.md` files and the exactness inventory) so it cannot collide with C6's still-open `interactions`/`forks` claim. **What landed:** `src/casim/index/` — one module per target, each reading a registry — plus `casim index [--check] [--only …]`. `code-index.md` goes from a docstring scrape to the module registry's own fields (sector, status, reach, findings, exactness, test dependents), and P6's kernel-coverage question becomes a generated cell: **47 of 194 registered modules are channel-driven (24%)**. `tests-index.md`'s filename-prefix heuristic — `result.startswith(test_stem)`, **capped at two matches**, empty on 104 of 339 rows — is **deleted, not improved**: every row is a test-registry record, so the mapping is declared, uncapped, and wrong only if a human wrote it wrong, in which case `casim test` fails on the same field. It carries two artifact columns on purpose: `Baselines` (the record's `results:`, what it fails on) and `Other artifacts` (files the results manifest links that the record does not declare — C7's arming to-do list for 52 records, in the open rather than dressed up as a mapping). `findings-index.md` gains the P0.3 annotations (ledger status, test-record count, number-reuse flag); `docs-index.md` now lists `deprecated/` (96 → 117 entries) because a superseded plan nobody can find is a plan someone rewrites. **`manifest.json`'s builder is imported, not re-implemented** — `tools/gen_manifest.py` is loaded by path and called, so the output is byte-identical and there is no second copy of the linking logic to drift (C5's precedent, importing `migrate_module.py`'s templates rather than copying them). **C8.2 — the finding-number audit found ten numbers used twice, not three:** F26 (speed-of-light-as-rotation-rate, which *is* CLAUDE.md core decision 2, vs bcc-spin-axis), F101, F102, F134, F136, F174, F176, F199, F200, F218 — and one of them is not a physics collision but a **filename defect hiding a second collision**: `F136-realspace-scalar-confinement.md` opens `# F135 — Real-space confinement (U1)`, so its content is F135 while `F135-blockspin-wavepacket-realtime.md` is a *different* F135; F135 is therefore also used twice, invisibly, because one of the pair wears an F136 filename. Gaps: F2–F15 **accepted** (findings 1–15 predate one-file-per-finding and live in `findings/F01-F15-findings.md`, which is also why the inventory cites "Finding 1" not "F1"), F219/F229 **resolved** (abandoned after collisions the changelog records), and **F111/F127 are real holes** — both have live tests (`test_F111_second_order_deflection.py` *and* `test_F111_tree_gauge_su3_ladder.py`, itself an F111 collision in the test tree, plus `test_F127_alpha_em_derivation.py`) and no finding file at all. All ten duplicates are recorded in the new `docs/design/finding-numbers.yaml` as **`status: unreviewed`**, and the count prints on every run: **detecting a collision is not resolving one**, deciding which file keeps a number is a physics-ownership call (F26 is a founding citation), so C8 turned an invisible problem into a listed one and stopped there. A *stale* declaration — an exception for a duplicate that no longer exists — also fails, because leaving one behind silently re-arms the next collision. **C8.4 closes P1.5 to 100% coverage honestly.** P1 could class 147 of 530 residual-bearing rows and said why (the artifact vocabulary is not standardised). C8 uses three rules in order and **records which fired per row**: `declared` (the entry's own label, P1's rule) **147**; `record` (the owning registry record's `expect.exactness`, D9, closed by construction) **0**; `signature` (the criterion the inventory document itself states in "Reading the table": 0 → exact, <1e-12 → machine, larger → quantitative) **383**. Coverage **530/530**, reported per rule rather than as one figure — 154 exact, 195 machine, 181 quantitative. **A correction to the C7 overview, made in both documents:** it claimed 84 records declared an `expect.exactness`; the true figure was **4** (the F234 record plus three scenarios — the only ones C7 hand-authored). The number was written from memory and not measured; C8 measured it at 4 on the first query, which is why rule 2 contributes nothing yet and rule 3 had to exist. What the field actually buys is that a declared class cannot be outside the closed vocabulary, not that many records had one. **The hand-written Tier 1–3 tables were NOT deleted**, against the roadmap's "generate them outright": they carry a `Predicted form` column — the physics claim — that no artifact records, so replacing them with machine labels like `C1_derived_cos3delta` would raise coverage and destroy information. They are retitled **(curated)** and stay authoritative for the claim; the generated tables are additive and complete. **Staleness reads 0 because the number is generated now, not because the metric was weakened** — the freshness header ("covers findings through **F265**") and the whole tally live inside generated markers, so the hand-typed `F140` that sat 125 findings behind cannot recur. **C8.3:** both old generators are deprecation shims that forward and re-implement nothing (removed at C9); `make indexes` / `make indexes-check` / `make manifest` all route through `casim index`; CLAUDE.md §"Index maintenance" rewritten, including the new rule that a finding-number collision must be declared; `INDEX.md` stays hand-maintained. **The gate loses two checks and gains one** — `gen_manifest.py --check` and `gen_exactness_inventory.py --check` become `casim index --check`, which answers for all seven targets plus the numbering audit, where before three separately-checked artifacts could each be stale while the others were clean. **A defect the check itself found:** `casim index --check` went red on a one-minute **mtime** difference, because the manifest records each artifact's mtime, so any run that merely *touched* a result file made it "stale" — which is exactly why `gen_manifest.py --check` kept reporting stale during C7 for no actionable reason. mtime is a filesystem fact, not content; the manifest already carries a numeric `fingerprint`, and that is what decides staleness now (`generated`, `git_sha`, `mtime` excluded). Idempotence is asserted, not eyeballed: the first implementation accumulated a blank line per run and `--check` caught it. New `tests/casim/test_index_integrity.py` (7 checks, gate tier, registry record, no import-time work) asserts idempotence, target coverage, no-empty-cell-the-registry-could-fill, staleness 0, per-rule coverage ≥95%, declared numbering, and that the code index still carries its registry columns. **Gate 16 of 17; `pytest tests/casim` 128 passed / 1 skipped; drift: none introduced** (the same 7 pre-existing artifacts as at C7 exit, all with mtimes before this session). The one red is the 52 C3.4 shim imports in `engine/interactions/` (23) and `engine/forks/` (29) — 52 at entry, 52 at exit, owned by the still-open C6 claim. **Needs a physics owner, not an engineer:** the ten unreviewed duplicate numbers (F26 and the F136/F135 filename fix first — one rename resolves two collisions), and F111/F127's missing finding files.
